# Tasks

Every task is self-contained: its **Read** list names what it needs, its **Depends on** line names what must
already exist, and its **Excludes** line names the nearby work that belongs to a different task — so it can be
planned and applied in a session that remembers nothing about the others. These rules apply to every task:

- **Size.** This change is far larger than the ~10-task norm (`AGENTS.md#how-work-is-planned`) because the owner asked
  for it as one change (proposal, assumption 1). Each numbered group is a mergeable unit, and the repository is green
  between groups: the last task of every group ends its **Done when** with `./gradlew build :app:archTest` — every
  module's tests, not only those of the modules the group touched — and any task that changes an `:api` shape, what a
  reader produces, or a bridge between modules runs `./gradlew build` itself rather than its own module's check. Only
  then does a group that depends on it start. Groups marked **∥** in their heading are independent of each other and
  may be worked in any order once their own **Depends on** is met; nothing in two ∥ groups edits the same file.
- **Git.** Work on `feature/complete-translation-workflow--<n.m>-<slug>` (for example `--1.1-project-records`), commit
  there, squash-merge one commit into `feature/complete-translation-workflow`, and delete the task branch
  (`AGENTS.md#git`). Never `--no-verify`, never force-push.
- **Tests first.** Write the task's tests, run them and watch them fail, then implement (`AGENTS.md#definition-of-done`).
  Name tests `method_state_expected`, put no `if`, `for` or `while` in a test body (use `@ParameterizedTest`), create
  files in `@TempDir`, and write expected values out instead of recomputing them (`.claude/rules/testing.md`). UI tests
  run on JavaFX 26's built-in headless platform (ADR-0019). "Wired" is asserted behaviourally: drive the state mirror and
  assert the node changed; fire the control and assert a recording fake port was called. No Mockito; WireMock only at
  the HTTP seam, for **both** dialects whenever a provider request changes. A fixture that asserts a segment is
  accepted passes every soft check at full margin under D-3 and the echo floor (below; `design.md` D8, D16): an en→uk
  reply is Cyrillic with a length ratio inside 0.81–1.69 of its source — `Маяк стояв на прибережній скелі.` (ratio 0.97)
  for a 33-character source, never `Маяк стояв на скелі.` (0.61) — while the pseudo model's upper-cased echo of a source
  of 20 or more code points is FLAGGED in every review mode and `Yes, sir.` → `YES, SIR.` is accepted at confidence 0.85.
- **Patterns to copy rather than invent.** TestFX: `modules/ui/src/test/java/ua/bookloom/ui/ShellTestBase.java`,
  `ScreenConformanceTest.java`, `screen/*ScreenTest.java`. Recording fakes: `modules/ui/src/test/java/ua/bookloom/ui/
  Scripted*.java`, `state/RecordingJob.java`, `modules/pipeline/src/test/java/ua/bookloom/pipeline/ScriptedChatModel.java`
  and `TranslationJobTestSupport.java`. WireMock: `modules/pipeline/src/test/java/ua/bookloom/pipeline/WireMockProvider.java`
  (pipeline e2e) and `modules/llm/src/test/java/ua/bookloom/llm/client/ollama/OllamaClientTest.java` /
  `.../openai/OpenAiCompatibleClientTest.java` (one server per dialect). Launch-flag resolution:
  `modules/app/src/main/java/ua/bookloom/app/bootstrap/LoggingLevelResolver.java`.
- **Logging is part of done.** Write what the task's **Log** line lists: INFO for lifecycle, DEBUG for every working
  method's parameters and every branch taken, TRACE for book text, prompts and replies, WARN for degraded, retried or
  refused outcomes, and never a credential (`.claude/rules/logging.md`). Job events carry book text; a log line above
  TRACE never does. No log call inside a layout pass, a cell's `updateItem`, or a listener on a high-frequency value.
- **Code rules.** `.claude/rules/java-coding-style.md`: records for data, `Objects.requireNonNull` at boundaries, a
  `@NullMarked` `package-info.java` with a purpose Javadoc for each new package, constructor injection only with
  `@RequiredArgsConstructor(onConstructor_ = {@Inject})` on anything Guice constructs implicitly, enum data in
  constructor fields, no `synchronized`, methods of at most 30 lines and files of at most 400.
  `.claude/rules/error-envelope.md`: every port method returns a `Result` behind a boundary `try/catch`; no new
  `ErrorCode` (ADR-0022, ADR-0039). `.claude/rules/javafx-ui.md` and `.claude/rules/theming-tokens.md`: no scene-graph
  access off the FX thread, no `node.setStyle(...)`, no colour outside a `.root` value block, every visible word from
  the message catalogue in **both** `messages_en` and `messages_uk`.
- **A new package is not done until JPMS knows about it.** A new `:api`/`:util` package is `exports`-ed; a new package
  holding a class Guice constructs gets `opens <package> to com.google.guice;` — a `:ui` package
  `opens <package> to javafx.fxml, com.google.guice;` — in the task that creates it (`design.md` D1); `:pipeline`'s
  prompt resources need no `opens`, because a module reads its own resources. A missing line passes every classpath
  test and fails only in the packaged image, which is why task 15.2 runs the packaging scripts.
- **Finish** with `./gradlew spotlessApply`, then the task's **Done when** commands.
- **Sources.** Requirements are named in quotes and live in this change's `specs/<capability>/spec.md`, or, for a
  shipped requirement this change leaves as it is, in `openspec/specs/<capability>/spec.md`. Decisions are `design.md`
  D1–D16 and ADR-0034 … ADR-0041. Every number the change fixes — thresholds, margins, caps, temperatures, timeouts —
  is listed once in `design.md` "Tuning constants"; a task that needs one takes it from there. The owner's decisions
  (proposal: What Changes, assumptions 6–8, Non-goals) bind every task:
  - **D-1** — the Providers tab's polish ships: the three test actions with measured badges (tasks 3.4, 14.2).
  - **D-2** — only the text values of Markdown frontmatter become segments: never a key, never `lang` or `language`,
    never a number, a boolean (YAML `yes`/`on` included), a null, a date, a URL, a list or a map; on export an existing
    top-level `lang` value is replaced by the target tag in its own quotes, and a `lang` key is never added.
  - **D-3** — a soft check that fails outright blocks acceptance: it raises a `medium` finding, so the segment is
    repaired and then FLAGGED; confidence decides only close calls.
  - **Echo floor** — a failed echo blocks only when the source's display text has at least 20 code points; below that it
    only lowers confidence (`It was a dark night.` is exactly 20 and blocks).
  - **D-4** — a TXT or Markdown export whose translation the source encoding cannot hold is written whole as UTF-8
    (ADR-0029), never refused; EPUB keeps its numeric character references and FB2 its existing switch.
  - **D-5** — names grow during the run: at each body unit's end a deterministic re-scan adds new unlocked candidates
    with no target, never changing an entry the person kept or bringing back one they removed.
  - **D-6** — kept foreign runs come from metadata only: under the Keep policy an inline element whose own
    `lang`/`xml:lang` differs from the source language is kept verbatim as one protected token.
  - **D-7** — no numeral masking and no automatic model pre-scan (the pre-scan runs only from its button); the reference
    clauses say so (group 0).
  - **D-8** — review stays a panel inside Translating, an approved departure from the mockup.

  The visual reference is `docs/specification/mockups/ui-mockup.html`; the crossed-out parts of
  `.temporary_context/screens_of_mockup/Mockup/*.png` (the "Design reference" group and the "Preview state" /
  run-state switcher bars) are never built.

there is no JSON array keyed by segment id and no id-mismatch fallback; cite ADR-0038); `#fr-export` FR-EXPORT-04 (the save path, Browse and overwrite are chosen on the Export screen, export runs at any time except while translating, a finished export is confirmed in a dialog; cite ADR-0036), FR-REVIEW-02 (τ comes from the review mode alone; the Settings override is not in this build), FR-REVIEW-05 (a retry replays the context the segment's first draft saw, runs no repair rounds, and is allowed whenever no run of the project is running — refused as busy, with no model call, while one is), FR-REVIEW-06 (Accept moves FLAGGED to ACCEPTED and confirms an ACCEPTED segment; on a PENDING or REVISED segment it is refused and changes nothing) and FR-REVIEW-09 (editing the target disables Accept until Save edit or Revert; Save edit records REVISED and keeps the machine target apart); `#fr-gloss` FR-GLOSS-01 (glossary proposals come from a deterministic frequency and capitalisation scan — when a run prepares on an empty glossary, when Names & style opens on an empty glossary, and at each body unit's end — unlocked, with no target and gender unknown; the LLM pre-scan runs only when the person presses its button, never at a run's start; owner decision D-7, cite DD-46); `#fr-resume` FR-RESUME-04 (a source file changed since the book was opened is detected by its hash when the book is exported, which is refused with `validation` and writes nothing; resuming within the session does not re-read the source) and FR-RESUME-05 (Stop aborts the request in flight and ends the run `stopped`, never as an error; progress is kept for the session and a new run for the same book starts at its first PENDING segment; nothing survives closing the application); `#fr-qa` FR-QA-03 (the target-language check is a script check — the share of letters in the target language's script, run only for sources of at least 20 characters between languages of different scripts — and under the Keep policy a segment whose declared block language differs from the run's source language, or whose dominant script differs from the source language's, has the echo and script checks skipped) and FR-QA-07 (a **segment**, not a chunk, is accepted when its hard gates pass, no soft check failed outright — a failed echo on a source under 20 code points only lowers confidence — confidence ≥ τ, and the judge is off or scored the chunk at τ_judge or above with no medium or high finding against that segment; a context-matched memory reuse is accepted without the judge; cite ADR-0038). In `docs/specification/01_Product/05_TRANSLATION_ALGORITHM.md`: `#phase-b-prep` FR-ALGO-B1 (preparation runs the deterministic scan when the glossary is empty; the LLM pre-scan is the person's button only, in batches of 40 candidates, merged only when every batch succeeded), `#token-budget` FR-ALGO-C1 (`chunkBudget = min(8192 − reservedHeadroom, 1200)`), `#phase-d-backward-revision` FR-ALGO-D2 (the deterministic sweep replaces a rendering only where the glossary held a previous target for that term, recorded when the person changed it; a rendering the model chose for a term that had no target is not known to the sweep). In `docs/specification/01_Product/03_DOCUMENT_FORMATS.md#drm-and-language-detection`, EC-LANG-1 and EC-LANG-2 say the same as FR-IMPORT-03 (metadata evidence, no text detection), EC-FOREIGN-1 says a block is foreign when it declares, on itself or an ancestor below the document root, a language other than the run's source language, or its dominant script differs from the source language's — nothing is detected from text — and the foreign-passage policy applies (keep by default), and EC-FOREIGN-3 says that under the Keep policy an inline element whose own `lang`/`xml:lang` differs from the source language is kept verbatim as one protected token while the prose around it is translated, and that an unmarked foreign run is not detected (owner decision D-6; cite ADR-0037). In `docs/specification/00_Foundation/04_DESIGN_DECISIONS.md`: `#dd-40-prompt-evals` (a note that `complete-translation-workflow` ships no `promptEval` harness — its prompts are pinned by a prompt-golden test and `liveLocal` cases — and the evals stay planned), `#dd-44-token-budget-heuristic` (the effective context is the fixed 8192 in this build; the per-provider resolution is not built), `#dd-45-acceptance-model` (the judge scores a chunk once and a segment is accepted only when its hard gates pass, no soft check failed outright — a failed check raises a medium finding and sends the segment to repair, the echo floor of 20 code points excepted — confidence reaches τ, and the chunk score reaches τ_judge with no medium or high finding against that segment; τ lives on the review mode; the manual Settings override is not in this build; cite ADR-0038, ADR-0036), `#dd-46-glossary-llm-pre-scan` (the deterministic scan seeds the glossary and grows it at each body unit's end; the LLM pre-scan runs only from its button and never at a run's start — owner decision D-7), `#dd-47-metadata-nav-alt-translation` (the auxiliary unit is always produced whatever the switches; descriptions — the EPUB `dc:description` and the FB2 `annotation` — are a `METADATA_DESCRIPTION` kind under the metadata switch; each XHTML page's `<head><title>` is a `TITLE` segment under the navigation switch; a switched-off kind is kept as source by choice; cite ADR-0041), `#dd-49-code-and-technical-content-preservation` (drop "selective numerals" from the masked list) and `#dd-30-export-original-format` (export is its own action, runnable at any time including on a partial book, still same-format only; cite ADR-0035). In `docs/specification/01_Product/02_TRANSLATION_WORKFLOW.md`: `#end-to-end-workflow` (the Names & style row: proposals come from the deterministic scan and grow during the run; the LLM pre-scan is a button, never automatic), `#review-modes` (the table's behaviour column says what each mode pauses on and its τ; Manual packs one segment per chunk) and `#workflow-states-and-recovery` (a provider error auto-pauses with Retry now; a user Stop keeps progress for the session; crash or quit loses the session until persistence exists; a changed source is detected at export, not before a resume). In `docs/specification/01_Product/07_SETTINGS.md`: `#generation-tab` — the `num_ctx` row and the chunk-budget paragraph say this build sends a fixed 8192 and uses no per-provider effective context, the 32768 default applying once the Generation tab is built; `#automation-tab` — the "Default review mode" row says the value comes from `BOOKLOOM_REVIEW_MODE` or `bookloom.review.mode` until Settings are persisted. In `docs/specification/01_Product/06_REVIEW_AND_EDITING.md#segment-actions`, add the Revert row, say that Accept acts on FLAGGED and ACCEPTED only and that Retry is allowed whenever no run of the project is running, and scope FR-REVIEW-A3 ("persists atomically") to the session.

the **echo floor**: below 20 code points of source display text a failed echo contributes its 0.0 margin and a `low` finding only; under the "keep original" name policy every whole-word glossary term is removed from both texts before the script share and the echo; length `clamp(distance to the nearer band bound / (0.10 × band width))`; glossary the fraction of in-segment locked terms rendered, 1.0 whenever the protected-span gate passed; the `m_targetLanguage` term becomes `m_script`; every repair entry (those four and `#reflect-critique`) carries exactly one `<Text>` block holding only the masked text to rewrite — the rejected target, or the masked source when the finding is a refusal or an empty target — with the source under a `[Source]` label outside it; Only text nodes change" invariant and the "Single-flight inference" invariant naming `tryAcquire`); `.claude/rules/document-roundtrip.md` (the first MUST, the masking MUST with its selective numerals and its "drop, add, or reorder inline structure" rationale, the "Placeholders are dropped, added, reordered" Reject-if line); `.claude/rules/threading-concurrency.md` (the `InferenceGate` MUST naming `tryAcquire`); `.claude/rules/llm-provider-integration.md` (the gate MUST naming `tryRun`, the credential pre-check MUST whose diagnostics "acquire the gate via `tryRun` (bounded wait)", the reasoning MUST "informed by `/api/show`", the effective-context SHOULD, and the Reject-if line "the gate blocks/queues instead of returning `busy`"); `.agents/skills/llm-provider-integration/SKILL.md` (step 8, the checklist and the Gotchas lines naming `tryRun`); `docs/specification/02_Architecture/08_THREADING_CONCURRENCY.md#gate-interplay`; `docs/specification/02_Architecture/04_LLM_INTEGRATION.md#inference-gate`; `docs/specification/01_Product/04_LLM_PROVIDERS_AND_MODELS.md#three-stage-verification` and `#provider-edge-cases` (EC-CONCUR-2); `modules/llm/src/main/java/ua/bookloom/llm/gate/InferenceGate.java` (only a blocking `run` exists) and `modules/llm/src/main/java/ua/bookloom/llm/verify/ProviderVerifierImpl.java` (verification goes through `gate.run`).
  - **Change:** `AGENTS.md` non-negotiables row → "Skeleton never regenerated; only text nodes and the attribute values DD-47 lists (image alt text) change", same enforcement column. `openspec/config.yaml`: the skeleton invariant → the same wording; the "Single-flight inference" invariant → one `InferenceGate` (`Semaphore(1)`) that blocks and guards all inference, runs and diagnostics alike — no call is refused by it; the one `busy` is a review retry asked while the project's run is RUNNING, refused from the run record and never queued behind the run (`design.md` D9) — keeping its backoff, retry and timeout sentences. `.claude/rules/document-roundtrip.md`: the first MUST names the alt exception and ADR-0041; the masking MUST masks inline tags, locked terms and URLs (no numerals, owner decision D-7) and says the gate checks the multiset **and** that paired tokens stay opening-before-closing and properly nested, that a pair whose source held text still holds text, and that a line-break token keeps its innermost enclosing pair, while an atomic token (image, code span, locked term, kept foreign run) may move (ADR-0040 as amended), with its rationale reworded to match; the Reject-if line "Placeholders are dropped, added, reordered" becomes "dropped, added, duplicated, a pair reversed or overlapped, a pair emptied of its text, or a line break moved across a pair boundary, instead of failing the hard gate". `.claude/rules/threading-concurrency.md`: the gate MUST says the semaphore blocks, a failed acquire does not exist, and a review retry is refused with `ErrorCode.busy` while the project's run is RUNNING and is never queued behind it. `.claude/rules/llm-provider-integration.md`: the gate MUST says `run` blocks and there is no `tryRun`, with the review retry's `busy` coming from the run record; the credential pre-check MUST says diagnostics go through the same blocking gate and wait for a call in flight; the Reject-if line becomes "Inference runs without acquiring the `InferenceGate`, or a review retry is queued behind a RUNNING run instead of being refused with `busy`"; the reasoning MUST loses "informed by `/api/show`" (the Ollama request carries `think:false` when reasoning is off and never reads `/api/show`); the effective-context SHOULD says this build sends one fixed context of 8192 as Ollama `num_ctx`, sends none to an OpenAI-compatible server, and resolves no per-provider value until the Generation settings exist. `.agents/skills/llm-provider-integration/SKILL.md`: step 8, the checklist line and the Gotchas line say the same (`run` only; `busy` from the run record for a review retry). `docs/specification/02_Architecture/08_THREADING_CONCURRENCY.md#gate-interplay`: the "Interactive 'retry now' uses `tryRun`" bullet says the same. `docs/specification/02_Architecture/04_LLM_INTEGRATION.md#inference-gate`: the sentence deferring "Interactive `tryRun` and the `ErrorCode.busy` UI behavior" says there is no `tryRun` and names the review retry's `busy`. `docs/specification/01_Product/04_LLM_PROVIDERS_AND_MODELS.md`: `#three-stage-verification`'s bounded-wait `tryRun` sentence and EC-CONCUR-2 in `#provider-edge-cases` say verification goes through the same blocking gate, so a test during a run waits for the call in flight.
  - **Test first:** none — documentation only; the greps under **Done when** are the check.
  - **Log:** none.

## 1. Contracts — after group 0 (docs only; may run alongside it)

- [ ] 1.1 Add the language catalogue and tag normalization to `:util` in a new exported package `ua.bookloom.util.lang`: the 34 languages the Book Brief offers, each with its tag, English display name, script and characters-per-token figure, and one `normalize` that folds case, reads `_` as `-`, maps the retired `ua` to `uk`, drops a region and splits Chinese into Simplified and Traditional. Import, the brief, the token estimator and the script check all need the same answer to "which language is this code"; books spell one language as `EN`, `en-GB`, `en_GB` or `ua`, and without one normalizer each caller would disagree (backlog decision debt D17). → `:util`
  - **Depends on:** nothing beyond the existing `:util` module.
  - **Excludes:** reading a book's declaration (task 4.1), display names in the interface language (task 11.8 derives them from ICU), and the token estimator itself (task 7.3), which only reads the characters-per-token figure.
  - **Read:** `specs/book-import/spec.md` "Normalize a declared language code before using it" (every scenario); `specs/book-brief/spec.md` "Offer one searchable list of languages for source and target" (the 34 names); `design.md` D1 (`util.lang`), D4 (K by script: Latin 4.0, Cyrillic 3.0, Greek 3.5, CJK 1.5, unknown 3.0), D11 (the list and the normalization rules) and "Tuning constants" (the token estimator row); ADR-0037; `docs/implementation_plan/CHANGE_BACKLOG.md#decision-debt` row D17; `modules/util/src/main/java/module-info.java`; `modules/util/src/main/java/ua/bookloom/util/paths/OsFamily.java` (enum data in constructor fields, the pattern to follow); `.claude/rules/java-coding-style.md` (enum data, `@NoArgsConstructor(access = AccessLevel.PRIVATE)` on a static utility).
  - **Change:** `ua.bookloom.util.lang.Script` — an enum `LATIN(4.0)`, `CYRILLIC(3.0)`, `GREEK(3.5)`, `HAN(1.5)`, `JAPANESE(1.5)`, `HANGUL(1.5)`, `UNKNOWN(3.0)`, each carrying its characters-per-token figure and the `Character.UnicodeScript` values that count as letters of it (`JAPANESE` = Han, Hiragana, Katakana) in constructor fields; there is no Arabic or Hebrew constant, because neither language is in the catalogue. `ua.bookloom.util.lang.Language` — an enum of exactly 34 constants carrying tag, English name and `Script`: `en` English, `bg` Bulgarian (Cyrillic), `hr` Croatian, `cs` Czech, `da` Danish, `nl` Dutch, `et` Estonian, `fi` Finnish, `fr` French, `de` German, `el` Greek (Greek), `hu` Hungarian, `ga` Irish, `it` Italian, `lv` Latvian, `lt` Lithuanian, `mt` Maltese, `pl` Polish, `pt` Portuguese, `ro` Romanian, `sk` Slovak, `sl` Slovenian, `es` Spanish, `sv` Swedish, `zh-Hans` Chinese (Simplified) (Han), `zh-Hant` Chinese (Traditional) (Han), `uk` Ukrainian, `ru` Russian, `be` Belarusian (all Cyrillic), `tr` Turkish, `ja` Japanese (Japanese), `nb` Norwegian Bokmål, `sr` Serbian (Cyrillic), `ko` Korean (Hangul); the rest Latin. `Languages` (static utility) with `all()` in that order and `byTag(String) → Optional<Language>`. `LanguageTags` (static utility) with `normalize(@Nullable String) → Optional<String>`: trim, treat `_` as `-`, lower-case the primary subtag, `ua`→`uk`; `zh`, `zh-CN`, `zh-SG`, `zh-Hans*` → `zh-Hans`; `zh-TW`, `zh-HK`, `zh-MO`, `zh-Hant*` → `zh-Hant`; otherwise drop every subtag after the primary one; empty when the result names no `Language`. `package-info.java` with `@NullMarked` and a purpose Javadoc; `exports ua.bookloom.util.lang;` in `modules/util/src/main/java/module-info.java`. No new dependency, no lockfile change.
  - **Test first:** unit tests in `modules/util/src/test/java/ua/bookloom/util/lang/` (created by this task). `LanguageTagsTest.normalize_*` as one `@ParameterizedTest` `@CsvSource`: `EN`→`en`, `en-US`→`en`, `en_GB`→`en`, `pt-BR`→`pt`, `ua`→`uk`, `UK`→`uk`, `zh`→`zh-Hans`, `zh-CN`→`zh-Hans`, `zh-SG`→`zh-Hans`, `zh_SG`→`zh-Hans`, `zh-TW`→`zh-Hant`, `zh-HK`→`zh-Hant`, `zh-MO`→`zh-Hant`, `zh-Hant-TW`→`zh-Hant`, `nb-NO`→`nb`, `" de "`→`de`; a second parameterized test for empty results: `xx-yy`, `xx`, `ar`, `he`, blank, `null` (`@NullAndEmptySource` plus values). `LanguagesTest`: `all()` holds exactly 34 entries with 34 distinct tags; `byTag("nb")` has the English name `Norwegian Bokmål`, `byTag("zh-Hant")` `Chinese (Traditional)`, `byTag("ga")` `Irish`; a `@CsvSource` of tag → script → figure: `uk`,`CYRILLIC`,`3.0`; `el`,`GREEK`,`3.5`; `ja`,`JAPANESE`,`1.5`; `ko`,`HANGUL`,`1.5`; `en`,`LATIN`,`4.0`; `sr`,`CYRILLIC`,`3.0`; `byTag("en-US")` is empty (lookup is by normalized tag only).
  - **Log:** none — pure lookups called once per segment by later code; the callers (tasks 4.1, 7.3, 8.1) log the decision with its inputs, and `.claude/rules/logging.md` keeps DEBUG out of pure queries (`docs/next_features.md` §12).

- [ ] 1.2 Add the project records to `:api` in a new exported package `ua.bookloom.api.project` — the project, the Book Brief with its enums and defaults, the "Also translate" switches with the auxiliary kinds they keep as source, the stored segment decision with its machine target and the user's edit each in plain and masked form and a reviewed mark, the per-status counts, QA findings, the context snapshot a retry replays (holding the texts the draft saw, not ids), the segment locator shown to people, glossary, translation-memory, summary, deferral and run records (the run's current state included), and the chunk commit — plus the `QualityDial` enum the brief carries, the `METADATA_DESCRIPTION` segment kind, and the auxiliary unit's id. Every storage port, the review desk, export and the reworked job read and write these shapes; the brief's defaults are what a newly opened book starts with, so getting one wrong silently changes every translation; and a record that kept only the plain user edit, or only whether a run had ended, would refuse a second edit of a formatted segment and a retry during a pause. → `:api`
  - **Depends on:** nothing beyond the existing `:api` module.
  - **Excludes:** the storage ports (task 1.3), the inspection records (task 1.4), the run, review-mode and event contracts (task 1.6), producing the auxiliary unit and its segments (tasks 6.2–6.5), and anything that stores or computes (groups 2, 7–10).
  - **Read:** `specs/book-brief/spec.md` "Capture the tone and style of the translation", "Capture the translation policies", "Choose which auxiliary text is also translated", "Choose the quality dial and show what it turns on", "Carry every brief choice into the run"; `specs/translation-pipeline/spec.md` "Skip the auxiliary text the brief switches off" (kept as source by choice, page titles under the navigation switch); `specs/review-queue/spec.md` "List flagged segments in a review panel inside the Translating screen" (the locator rule), "Move segments only through the segment status machine", "Accept, save an edit, revert, and never discard an edit silently", "Retry a segment with the context it first saw", "Apply backward-revision proposals for edited segments only on acceptance"; `specs/resume/spec.md` "Record whether a run is running, paused or ended"; `specs/glossary/spec.md` "Show the glossary as an editable table on Names & style" (the types and genders), "Import and export the glossary as CSV" (the columns); `specs/quality-gates/spec.md` "Record each segment's findings for review and repair"; `specs/document-round-trip/spec.md` "Give every unit a stable identity appropriate to its format" (the id `aux`), "Produce a book's auxiliary text as translatable segments" (the description kind); `design.md` D1, D2 (the record shapes, masked forms, kept as source by choice, run state), D10, D12 (`METADATA_DESCRIPTION`, the "does not grow" note); ADR-0034; `docs/specification/02_Architecture/06_DATA_MODEL_SQLITE.md#ddl-normative` (the columns these records map onto); `modules/api/src/main/java/ua/bookloom/api/document/{Segment,SegmentStatus,SegmentKind,BookFormat,Unit}.java`; `modules/api/src/main/java/ua/bookloom/api/pipeline/JobState.java`; `modules/api/src/main/java/module-info.java`.

In `ua.bookloom.api.project`: enums `Register {FORMAL_LITERARY, NEUTRAL, CASUAL}`, `NamePolicy {TRANSLATE, TRANSLITERATE, KEEP_ORIGINAL}`, `ForeignPassagePolicy {KEEP, TRANSLATE, TRANSLATE_WITH_NOTE}`, `FootnotePolicy {TRANSLATE, KEEP}`, `UnitPolicy {KEEP, METRIC}`, `SegmentPath {DRAFT, TM_REUSE, REPAIRED, USER, SOURCE_KEPT}` (`SOURCE_KEPT` is never stored; review views report it for a record kept as source by choice), `TermType {CHARACTER, PLACE, TERM, TITLE, OTHER}`, `Gender {FEMALE, MALE, NEUTER, UNKNOWN}`, `Severity {LOW, MEDIUM, HIGH}`, `DeferralReason {GENDER_UNKNOWN, NAME_UNRESOLVED, JUDGE, TERM}` (`TERM`: a locked rendering the person changed after decided segments used the old one, task 9.7); and in `ua.bookloom.api.pipeline` the enum `QualityDial {FAST, BALANCED, MAX}` (the brief needs it now; task 1.6 uses it). Records: `AlsoTranslate(boolean navigationLabels, boolean altText, boolean metadata, boolean frontmatter)` with `defaults()` = on, on, on, off, `covers(SegmentKind auxiliaryKind) → boolean` answering whether a run translates an auxiliary segment of that kind — `NAV_LABEL` and `TITLE` (a page title) → navigation labels; `ALT` → alt text; `METADATA_TITLE`, `METADATA_AUTHOR`, `METADATA_DESCRIPTION` → metadata; `FRONTMATTER_VALUE` → frontmatter; any other kind → `true` — and `keptKinds() → Set<SegmentKind>`, the auxiliary kinds `covers` answers false for, as an unmodifiable copy; its Javadoc says only records of the auxiliary unit consult it, so a body `TITLE` is always translated (`design.md` D2); `SegmentCounts(int pending, int accepted, int revised, int flagged, int sourceKept)` rejecting a negative count — what `SegmentRepository.countsByStatus` answers (task 1.3), a record kept as source by choice counted only as `sourceKept`; `BookBrief(@Nullable String sourceLanguage, @Nullable String targetLanguage, @Nullable String genre, Register register, @Nullable String voiceEra, @Nullable String audience, NamePolicy names, ForeignPassagePolicy foreignPassages, FootnotePolicy footnotes, UnitPolicy units, int balance, AlsoTranslate alsoTranslate, QualityDial dial)` rejecting a balance outside 0–100, with `defaults(@Nullable String sourceLanguage)`; `Project(String id, Path source, BookFormat format, String contentHash, BookBrief brief)` with `withBrief(BookBrief)`; `QaFinding(String kind, Severity severity, String note)`; `SnapshotTerm(String term, @Nullable String target, TermType type, Gender gender, boolean locked)`; `SnapshotTmHit(TmHitKind kind, String source, String target)` with enum `TmHitKind {EXACT, CONTEXT, FUZZY}`; `ContextSnapshot(List<String> precedingTargets, List<SnapshotTerm> glossary, List<SnapshotTmHit> tmHits, @Nullable String summary, String styleSheet)` — the **texts** the draft saw, so Retry replays exactly with no repository lookup even after the glossary, memory or summary changed; `SegmentRecord(String projectId, String segmentId, String unitId, int ord, SegmentKind kind, SegmentStatus status, @Nullable String machineTarget, @Nullable String maskedMachineTarget, @Nullable String userTarget, @Nullable String maskedUserTarget, double confidence, @Nullable Double judgeScore, List<QaFinding> findings, SegmentPath path, int repairRounds, boolean reviewed, @Nullable ContextSnapshot context)` — `machineTarget` is the last target that passed every hard gate; the masked forms are the text after protected-span restore and before `DocumentPort.unmask` (the document's own `⟦gN⟧` tokens still in place, locked names shown as their renderings), which is what the review editor shows and saves (`design.md` D2); `reviewed` is true once a person accepted, saved, reverted, applied a proposal or retried successfully — with `effectiveTarget() → Optional<String>` (the user's edit when present, else the machine target), `isKeptAsSource(Set<SegmentKind> keptAuxiliaryKinds) → boolean` (true exactly for a record of the auxiliary unit whose kind is in the set, whatever its status — the one rule every repository read and count applies), and `with…` copy methods for status, machine targets (plain and masked together), user targets (plain and masked together), findings, path and reviewed; `SegmentLocator(String text)` (a record, so `:api` stays free of Lombok and every framework) with the factory `static SegmentLocator of(SegmentKind kind, int unitOrdinal, int segmentOrdinal)` — `unitOrdinal` 0 is the auxiliary unit and body units count from 1; a body segment reads `ch<n> · p<mm>` with `n` its unit's position among body units and `mm` its 1-based position in its unit, zero-padded to two digits (`ch5 · p12`, `ch1 · p03`); an auxiliary segment reads by its kind — `METADATA_TITLE` → `title`, `METADATA_AUTHOR` → `author · <kk>`, `METADATA_DESCRIPTION` → `description · <kk>`, `NAV_LABEL` → `nav · <kk>` (NCX labels included), `TITLE` → `page title · <kk>`, `ALT` → `alt · <kk>`, `FRONTMATTER_VALUE` → `frontmatter · <kk>`, with `kk` = `segmentOrdinal`, its 1-based position among the auxiliary segments of that kind, two digits — the one rule the review panel, toasts, the log and the export report use (`design.md` D2); `GlossaryEntry(String id, String projectId, String term, @Nullable String target, TermType type, Gender gender, boolean locked)`; `TmEntry(String id, String projectId, String sourceHash, String contextKey, String sourceInner, String targetInner)`; `RollingSummary(String projectId, @Nullable String unitId, String source, String target, int version, @Nullable String lastSummarizedKey, int tokensSince)`; `Deferral(String id, String projectId, String segmentId, DeferralReason reason, @Nullable String waitingOn, @Nullable String replacedRendering, @Nullable String proposal, @Nullable String maskedProposal)` (the rendering a locked-term sweep replaces, and the proposal shown for a user-edited segment in both forms, so applying it stores both user targets — tasks 9.7, 10.7); `RunRecord(String runId, String projectId, Instant startedAt, @Nullable Instant endedAt, JobState state, int accepted, int flagged)` — `state` is the run's **current** state, `RUNNING`, `PAUSED`, or the terminal `COMPLETED`, `CANCELLED` or `FAILED`, never `NEW`, with `endedAt` present exactly when the state is terminal (`design.md` D2; review reads it to allow a retry, task 10.6); `ChunkCommit(String projectId, List<SegmentRecord> segments, List<TmEntry> tmEntries, List<GlossaryEntry> glossaryAdditions, List<Deferral> deferrals)`. `DeferralReason.NAME_UNRESOLVED`, `Deferral.waitingOn` and `RollingSummary.lastSummarizedKey`/`tokensSince` mirror columns of `docs/specification/02_Architecture/06_DATA_MODEL_SQLITE.md#ddl-normative` (ADR-0034's column-for-column mapping) and nothing in this change produces them. Every record: `Objects.requireNonNull` on non-nullable components, lists copied with `List.copyOf`. `package-info.java` with `@NullMarked` and a purpose Javadoc; `exports ua.bookloom.api.project;`. No logging (`:api` holds contracts only).

a `@ValueSource(ints = {-1, 101})` case throws `IllegalArgumentException`; `0` and `100` are accepted. `AlsoTranslateTest.covers_*` as a `@CsvSource` over kind and a switch set: `ALT` with alt text off → false; - [ ] 1.3 Add the storage ports to `:api` in a new exported package `ua.bookloom.api.persistence` — project, segment, glossary, translation-memory, summary, deferral and run repositories and one checkpoint port that commits a chunk's decided segments — every method returning `Result`, with the segment reads taking the auxiliary kinds the brief keeps as source, the glossary remembering a term the person removed, runs saved by upsert, and a commit that skips a glossary addition instead of failing. Review, export, retry and resume must read one source of truth that SQLite can later replace without touching a caller (ADR-0034); a port that throws or returns a bare value would make the in-memory adapter the only possible implementation, and a commit that failed on a duplicate name would throw away a chunk's decisions because the person added that name during a pause. → `:api`
  - **Depends on:** task 1.2 (the records these ports carry, `SegmentCounts` and `SegmentRecord.isKeptAsSource` included).
  - **Excludes:** the in-memory adapters and the contract test that is the ports' executable specification (group 2); service ports such as `ProjectService` (task 1.6).
  - **Read:** ADR-0034 (ports in `:api`, commits of decided segments, one book keyed as if many); `design.md` D2 (the reads the screens and the job need, kept as source by choice, commits, the removal memory, the run state); `specs/resume/spec.md` "Keep a run's progress only until the application closes", "Record whether a run is running, paused or ended"; `specs/review-queue/spec.md` "Move segments only through the segment status machine", "Allow a retry whenever no run of the project is running"; `specs/translation-pipeline/spec.md` "Reuse the translation memory only where its context matches", "Skip the auxiliary text the brief switches off"; `specs/glossary/spec.md` "Add a term through a dialog that refuses duplicates", "Keep the person's entries and removals when names are proposed"; `.claude/rules/error-envelope.md`; `modules/api/src/main/java/ua/bookloom/api/{Result,AppError,ErrorCode}.java`.

`SegmentRepository` (`saveAll(String projectId, List<SegmentRecord>) → Result<Integer>` — document order is the order given; `find(projectId, segmentId) → Result<Optional<SegmentRecord>>`; `firstPending(String projectId, Set<SegmentKind> keptAuxiliaryKinds) → Result<Optional<SegmentRecord>>` — the first PENDING record in document order that is not kept as source; `countsByStatus(String projectId, Set<SegmentKind> keptAuxiliaryKinds) → Result<SegmentCounts>` — every record counted once, a record kept as source only as `sourceKept` whatever its status; `flagged(String projectId, Set<SegmentKind> keptAuxiliaryKinds) → Result<List<SegmentRecord>>` in document order, kept records left out; `byUnit(projectId, unitId) → Result<List<SegmentRecord>>`; `all(projectId) → Result<List<SegmentRecord>>`; `update(projectId, segmentId, UnaryOperator<SegmentRecord>) → Result<SegmentRecord>`, atomic per record, `validation` for an unknown id) — "kept as source" is `SegmentRecord.isKeptAsSource` with the set the caller passes; `TmRepository` (`put(TmEntry)` — one entry per project, source hash and context key; `exact(projectId, sourceHash) → Result<List<TmEntry>>`; `context(projectId, sourceHash, contextKey) → Result<Optional<TmEntry>>`; `DeferralRepository` (`add(Deferral)` — idempotent on project, segment and reason; `open(projectId)`; `resolve(projectId, deferralId)`); `RunRepository` (`save(RunRecord) → Result<RunRecord>` — an upsert by run id, so every state change of one run lands in one record; `latest(projectId) → Result<Optional<RunRecord>>` — the run started last); `CheckpointPort` (`commit(ChunkCommit) → Result<Integer>` — the segment records, TM entries and deferrals all applied or none; the count is what was applied). ## 2. ∥ In-memory storage — after 1.2 and 1.3

- [ ] 2.1 Implement the project and segment repositories in memory in a new package `ua.bookloom.persistence.memory`, over one shared store, and write the abstract `RepositoryContractTest` against the ports so the SQLite change reuses it unchanged. Every screen and the job read the segment repository — first pending, counts by status, the flagged list, one unit's segments — and each read must leave out, or count apart, the auxiliary segments the brief keeps as source by choice; these reads must be correct and atomic before anything is built on them. → `:persistence`
  - **Depends on:** tasks 1.2 and 1.3.
  - **Excludes:** the glossary, TM, summary, deferral and run repositories (task 2.2), the chunk checkpoint and its locking (task 2.3), Guice bindings and `module-info` (task 2.4); deciding which kinds are kept (the brief's `AlsoTranslate.keptKinds()`, read by the job and the screens).
  - **Read:** ADR-0034; `design.md` D2 (concurrent maps, atomic per-key updates, kept as source by choice, the shared contract test); `specs/review-queue/spec.md` "Move segments only through the segment status machine" ("A flagged segment is not picked up again by the run"); `specs/translation-pipeline/spec.md` "Skip the auxiliary text the brief switches off" ("A switch turned off after a run keeps its segments as source"); `specs/resume/spec.md` "Keep a run's progress only until the application closes"; the two port files `modules/api/src/main/java/ua/bookloom/api/persistence/{ProjectRepository,SegmentRepository}.java` (task 1.3); `modules/api/src/main/java/ua/bookloom/api/project/SegmentRecord.java` (task 1.2; `isKeptAsSource`); `modules/persistence/build.gradle.kts` (the test conventions are applied; the module has no `src/test` yet); `.claude/rules/error-envelope.md` (boundary `try/catch` on every port method); `.claude/rules/testing.md`.
  - **Change:** `InMemoryStore` (package-private state holder, one per injector): per-project maps of projects and segment records, each segment remembering its insertion index as its document order, and one `ReentrantReadWriteLock` that multi-record reads take for reading and task 2.3's checkpoint takes for writing. `InMemoryProjectRepository` and `InMemorySegmentRepository` implement the ports over it; `firstPending`, `countsByStatus` and `flagged` apply `SegmentRecord.isKeptAsSource` with the set the caller passes, never a set of their own; `update` uses the map's `compute`; unknown project or segment → `Result.err(validation)`; any escaping `Throwable` → `Result.err(internal)`; constructors `@RequiredArgsConstructor(onConstructor_ = {@Inject})`. `package-info.java` with `@NullMarked` and a purpose Javadoc. The contract test lives in `modules/persistence/src/test/java/ua/bookloom/persistence/contract/RepositoryContractTest.java` (abstract, with factory methods for each port; created by this task) and `modules/persistence/src/test/java/ua/bookloom/persistence/memory/InMemoryRepositoryContractTest.java` (created by this task) extends it — the SQLite adapter will live in the same module, so the test source set is shared without a test-fixtures plugin, which no module in this build uses.
  - **Test first:** in the contract test (repository contract tier), with the records `ch1:0`, `ch1:1` (unit `ch1`), `ch2:0` (unit `ch2`), `aux:title` (unit `aux`, `METADATA_TITLE`) and `aux:fm:title` (unit `aux`, `FRONTMATTER_VALUE`) saved PENDING in that order and the kept set `{FRONTMATTER_VALUE}`: a saved project is found by id and an unknown id answers ok-and-empty; `firstPending` = `ch1:0`; after `ch1:0` is updated to ACCEPTED, `firstPending` = `ch1:1` and `countsByStatus` = `SegmentCounts(pending=3, accepted=1, revised=0, flagged=0, sourceKept=1)`; after `ch1:1` becomes FLAGGED, `flagged` returns exactly `[ch1:1]` and `firstPending` = `ch2:0` (a flagged segment is never picked up again); after `ch2:0` is ACCEPTED, `firstPending` = `aux:title` and never `aux:fm:title`; read with the empty kept set, `firstPending` after `aux:title` is ACCEPTED is `aux:fm:title`; `aux:fm:title` made FLAGGED and read again with `{FRONTMATTER_VALUE}` is absent from `flagged` and counted only in `sourceKept`; `byUnit("ch1")` returns `ch1:0`, `ch1:1` in order; updating unknown `ch9:0` answers `ErrorCode.validation`; records of project `p1` are invisible to project `p2`. Concurrency: two threads each applying 1,000 updates that increment `repairRounds` on `ch1:0` end at `2000`.
  - **Log:** DEBUG on each write (project id, segment id or count, the status moved from and to) and on each branch that answers an error (the id and the code); no DEBUG in the pure reads (`find`, `countsByStatus`, `flagged`, `firstPending`), which run per segment (`docs/next_features.md` §12); never a target text above TRACE.
  - **Done when:** `./gradlew :persistence:check` is green with branch coverage at or above the core floor.

- [ ] 2.2 Implement the glossary, translation-memory, summary, deferral and run repositories in memory over the same store, with case-insensitive glossary terms and a memory of the terms the person removed, one TM entry per source hash and context key, exact, context and fuzzy-candidate TM lookups, and runs saved by upsert so each run's current state is one record. The glossary's duplicate rule is what the Add term dialog relies on and its removal memory is what stops the chapter-end scan bringing a deleted name back; the TM lookups decide whether a segment is reused without a model call — a wrong key here silently reuses a translation in the wrong context; and a review retry is allowed or refused from the latest run's state. → `:persistence`
  - **Depends on:** task 2.1 (the store and the contract test).
  - **Excludes:** the checkpoint (task 2.3); fuzzy similarity scoring and context-key hashing (task 9.4 in `:pipeline`) — the repository only filters candidates by source length; writing the run states during a run (tasks 10.1, 10.4).
  - **Read:** `specs/glossary/spec.md` "Add a term through a dialog that refuses duplicates", "Import and export the glossary as CSV", "Keep the person's entries and removals when names are proposed"; `specs/translation-pipeline/spec.md` "Reuse the translation memory only where its context matches", "Keep a rolling summary of the book so far", "Record deferrals and revise backwards on Max"; `specs/resume/spec.md` "Record whether a run is running, paused or ended"; `design.md` D2 (the removal memory, the run upsert), D10; `docs/specification/02_Architecture/06_DATA_MODEL_SQLITE.md#ddl-normative` (the `UNIQUE` constraints of `glossary`, `tm`, `deferrals`); the five port files in `modules/api/src/main/java/ua/bookloom/api/persistence/` (task 1.3).
  - **Change:** `InMemoryGlossaryRepository` (term uniqueness by `toLowerCase(Locale.ROOT)` per project, insertion order kept; `remove` records the lower-cased term in a per-project removed set, `wasRemoved` reads it, `add` clears it for that term), `InMemoryTmRepository` (key: project, source hash, context key; `put` replaces), `InMemorySummaryRepository` (latest by highest version), `InMemoryDeferralRepository` (idempotent on project, segment, reason), `InMemoryRunRepository` (upsert by run id; `latest` = the record with the latest `startedAt`); each with the boundary `try/catch` and `@RequiredArgsConstructor(onConstructor_ = {@Inject})`. The contract test gains the matching abstract factory methods.
  - **Test first:** contract-test cases: adding `Hale` then `hale` answers `ErrorCode.validation` and leaves one entry; `findByTerm("HALE")` finds `Hale`; updating its target to `Гейл` is kept; removing it empties the list and `wasRemoved(p1, "HALE")` is true; adding `hale` again clears the memory (`wasRemoved` false) and holds one entry; a removal in `p1` is not remembered for `p2`. TM: `put(h1, c1, "Hello", "Привіт")` and `put(h1, c2, "Hello", "Вітаю")` give `exact(h1)` of size 2 and `context(h1, c2)` = `Вітаю`; a second `put(h1, c1, …, "Здоров")` replaces the first (still 2 entries); `candidates(p1, 3, 6)` returns the entries whose source is 3–6 characters and excludes a 20-character one. Summary: versions 1 then 2 saved → `latest` is version 2. Deferral: adding `(ch1:4, GENDER_UNKNOWN)` twice keeps one; `resolve` removes it from `open`. Run (resume "Record whether a run is running, paused or ended"): saving run `r1` RUNNING, then `r1` PAUSED, `r1` RUNNING and `r1` COMPLETED with an end time leaves one record whose state `latest(p1)` reports after each save as RUNNING, PAUSED, RUNNING, COMPLETED ("The state follows pauses and resumes"); a run saved CANCELLED reads as stopped ("A stopped run is recorded as stopped") and one saved FAILED as failed ("A failed run is recorded as failed"); a run `r2` started after `r1` is `latest`.
  - **Log:** DEBUG on each write (project id, entry id, term length or key hashes, the decision taken — added, replaced, refused as duplicate, removal remembered or cleared; run id and state); no DEBUG in pure reads; never a term, source or target text above TRACE.
  - **Done when:** `./gradlew :persistence:check` is green.

- [ ] 2.3 Implement `InMemoryCheckpoint.commit(ChunkCommit)` so a chunk's segment records, TM entries, deferrals and new glossary names become visible all together or not at all, skipping a glossary addition whose term already exists or was removed by the person, refusing only a segment the project does not hold, and prove it with a two-thread visibility test. A reader such as the review panel or a progress count that saw half a chunk would show a segment ACCEPTED whose TM entry does not yet exist, a failure in the middle would leave the book in a state no resume can explain (ADR-0034, DD-20), and a commit that failed because the person added a name during a pause would throw away the chunk's decisions. → `:persistence`
  - **Depends on:** tasks 2.1 and 2.2.
  - **Excludes:** bindings (task 2.4); deciding what goes into a commit (tasks 10.1–10.3).
  - **Read:** ADR-0034 (as amended: commits hold decided segments); `design.md` D2 (one lock so a commit is never half-visible; the commit skips an existing or removed term); `specs/glossary/spec.md` "Keep the person's entries and removals when names are proposed" ("A chapter-end scan leaves the person's entry alone", "A removed term is not proposed again"); `specs/translation-pipeline/spec.md` "Propose new names at the end of each body unit"; `docs/specification/02_Architecture/08_THREADING_CONCURRENCY.md` (no `synchronized`); `modules/api/src/main/java/ua/bookloom/api/persistence/CheckpointPort.java` (task 1.3); `modules/api/src/main/java/ua/bookloom/api/project/ChunkCommit.java` (task 1.2).
  - **Change:** `InMemoryCheckpoint` takes the store's write lock, validates first that every segment record exists and belongs to the commit's project, then applies the segment records, TM entries and deferrals, and adds each glossary addition whose term the project neither holds (ignoring case) nor has in its removed set — skipping the others with the existing entry untouched — releasing in `finally`; an unknown segment applies nothing and answers `ErrorCode.validation`; the answer is the number of records applied (segments, TM entries, deferrals and glossary entries added); the multi-record reads of task 2.1 already take the read lock.
  - **Test first:** repository contract tier: a commit of two segment updates, one TM entry and one deferral answers `4` and all four are then readable; a commit whose second segment id is unknown answers `validation` and leaves the first segment PENDING and no TM entry stored; a commit adding the glossary term `Hale` when the person's `hale` → `Гейл` (character, male, locked) exists applies its segments and TM entry, keeps that entry exactly as it was, adds no second entry and answers the count without the skipped addition; a commit adding `Chapter` after the person removed `Chapter` in this session applies its segments and adds no `Chapter`; a commit adding the new term `Milton` adds it unlocked with no target. Concurrency: a writer thread commits 500 chunks, each moving two segments PENDING → ACCEPTED together, while a reader thread calls `countsByStatus` 5,000 times — every observed accepted count is even (no half-applied chunk is ever seen). Written against the port in `RepositoryContractTest`.
  - **Log:** DEBUG per commit (project id, counts of each list, how many glossary additions were skipped as existing or removed, the outcome); DEBUG on a refused commit naming the first offending id and the code; the skipped terms at TRACE only; INFO nowhere (a commit is per chunk, not a lifecycle event).
  - **Done when:** `./gradlew :persistence:check` is green and `grep -rn "synchronized" modules/persistence/src/main` prints nothing.

The ports are useless until something can inject them, and a missing `opens` passes every classpath test and fails only in the packaged image (`AGENTS.md#what-will-bite-you`). → `:persistence`, `:app`

## 3. ∥ Provider clients — after 1.5

- [ ] 3.1 Make the Ollama-native client read token usage from `prompt_eval_count`, `eval_count` and `eval_duration`, and send the request's context size as `options.num_ctx`, leaving the `options` object out only when neither a temperature nor a context size is given, and prove that a request `GatedChatModel` sends again without a rejected control or format still posts that context size. Ollama's default context silently truncates a long prompt (proposal assumption 4), a downgraded retry that lost `num_ctx` would bring that truncation back, and without usage figures the Translating screen can only estimate its speed. → `:llm`
  - **Depends on:** task 1.5 (`TokenUsage`, `ChatResponse.usage`, `ChatRequest.contextWindow`, the copy methods `GatedChatModel` now uses).
  - **Excludes:** the OpenAI-compatible dialect (task 3.2), the timeout (task 3.3), the engine's choice of 8192 (task 7.3).
  - **Read:** `specs/llm-provider/spec.md` "Shape an Ollama-native chat request" ("A full request", "A context size alone", "Absent settings are left out"), "Read a reply and report its finish" (the Ollama usage scenario); `specs/inference/spec.md` "Carry the provider's token usage on a reply", "Carry a temperature and a response format per call", "Request provider controls without trusting them as guarantees" ("An unsupported native thinking control is omitted once", "A request retried without structured output still carries its context size"); `design.md` D6; `modules/llm/src/main/java/ua/bookloom/llm/dto/{OllamaChatRequest,OllamaChatResponse}.java` (the compact constructor that drops `options` when the temperature is null); `modules/llm/src/main/java/ua/bookloom/llm/client/ollama/OllamaClient.java` (`serializeRequest`, `chatResponse`); `modules/llm/src/test/java/ua/bookloom/llm/client/ollama/OllamaClientTest.java` (one WireMock server per dialect); `modules/llm/src/test/java/ua/bookloom/llm/GatedChatModelTest.java` (the two downgrade tests); `modules/llm/src/test/java/ua/bookloom/llm/dto/DtoSerializationTest.java`.
  - **Change:** `OllamaChatRequest.Options` gains `@JsonProperty("num_ctx") @Nullable Integer numCtx`; the compact constructor drops `options` only when both fields are null. `OllamaChatResponse` gains `prompt_eval_count`, `eval_count` (`@Nullable Integer`) and `eval_duration` (`@Nullable Long`, nanoseconds). `OllamaClient` copies `contextWindow` into `numCtx` and builds `TokenUsage` from the three fields when at least one is present (`Duration.ofNanos`), else no usage.
  - **Test first:** WireMock at `/api/chat` in `OllamaClientTest`: a request with temperature `0.2` and context size `8192` posts `"options":{"temperature":0.2,"num_ctx":8192}`; context size alone posts `"options":{"num_ctx":8192}`; neither posts no `options` key; a reply with `"prompt_eval_count":812,"eval_count":96,"eval_duration":3200000000` carries usage 812 / 96 / 3.2 s; a reply with none of the three carries null usage; a reply with only `"eval_count":40` carries completion 40 and null prompt and generation. `GatedChatModelTest` (WireMock at `/api/chat`): Ollama rejecting `"think":false` on a request carrying the context size `8192` → the next posted body has the same messages, temperature and format, `"num_ctx":8192` and no `think`; Ollama rejecting the JSON-schema format of a request carrying temperature `0.2` and context size `8192` → the next posted body has no `format` and `"options":{"temperature":0.2,"num_ctx":8192}`. `DtoSerializationTest`: the options object serializes `num_ctx` and omits a null temperature.
  - **Log:** DEBUG the request shape (model, message count, temperature present, context size, format present) and the usage figures read (counts and generation milliseconds — numbers, never text); TRACE the request and reply bodies as today.
  - **Done when:** `./gradlew :llm:check` is green.

- [ ] 3.2 Make the OpenAI-compatible client read `usage.prompt_tokens` and `usage.completion_tokens`, time the call by the wall clock through an injectable time source, and never send a context-size field under any name. LM Studio reports token counts but no generation time, so the whole call's duration stands in for it; a strict server rejects a field it does not know, so the context size that Ollama needs must never reach it. → `:llm`
  - **Depends on:** task 1.5.
  - **Excludes:** the Ollama dialect (task 3.1), the timeout (task 3.3).

  - **Change:** `OpenAiChatResponse` gains `@Nullable Usage usage` with `prompt_tokens` and `completion_tokens`; `OpenAiCompatibleClient` receives a monotonic time source (`LongSupplier` of nanoseconds, `System::nanoTime` in `LlmModule`, passed through `ProviderClientFactory`) and measures the HTTP exchange; usage = counts plus the measured duration when either count is present, else null. The request DTO is unchanged and the client ignores `contextWindow`.
  - **Test first:** WireMock at `/v1/chat/completions` in `OpenAiCompatibleClientTest`: a request with temperature `0.2` and context size `8192` for `google/gemma-4-e4b` posts `"temperature":0.2` and none of `num_ctx`, `n_ctx`, `context_length`, `options` (asserted with JSON-path absence on the recorded body); a reply with `"usage":{"prompt_tokens":640,"completion_tokens":75,"total_tokens":715}` and a scripted time source answering `0` then `2_500_000_000` carries usage 640 / 75 / 2.5 s; a reply with no `usage` object carries null usage.

- [ ] 3.3 Replace the fixed per-request timeout of a chat call with one scaled to the request's expected output in both clients — `max(configured, min(600 s, expected output tokens × 0.5 s))`, the configured request timeout (180 s by default, or the command line's `--timeout`) always the floor — keeping the configured timeout for a request that states no expected output. A fixed three-minute wait can be too short for a page from a slow model, while cutting a short call below the configured timeout would time out the first call after Ollama reloads a model for a larger context, or a prompt read on a CPU, and pause the run for nothing (`docs/next_features.md` §15). → `:llm`
  - **Depends on:** task 1.5 (`ChatRequest.expectedOutputTokens`, kept on a downgraded request).

  - **Read:** `specs/llm-provider/spec.md` "Scale a chat call's timeout with its expected output" (every scenario, "The command line's timeout is the floor" and "A long configured timeout is never cut" included); `specs/inference/spec.md` "Request provider controls without trusting them as guarantees" ("A request retried without structured output still carries its context size" — its attempt waits 200 s); `design.md` D6 and "Tuning constants" (the chat timeout row); `modules/llm/src/main/java/ua/bookloom/llm/http/{HttpExchange,HttpErrorMapper}.java` (the timeout detail comes from `config.requestTimeout()`); `modules/api/src/main/java/ua/bookloom/api/llm/ProviderConfig.java` (`withRequestTimeout`, `DEFAULT_REQUEST_TIMEOUT` = 3 minutes); `modules/llm/src/test/java/ua/bookloom/llm/http/HttpExchangeTest.java` (`get_delayedResponseMapsRequestTimeoutWithSafeTimeoutDetail`, the delay pattern); `modules/llm/src/test/java/ua/bookloom/llm/GatedChatModelTest.java` (`chat_structuredOutputRejected_retriesOnceWithoutResponseFormat`).
  - **Change:** `ua.bookloom.llm.http.RequestTimeouts` (static utility) with `forChat(ProviderConfig config, @Nullable Integer expectedOutputTokens) → ProviderConfig`: when an expectation is present, `config.withRequestTimeout(max(config.requestTimeout(), min(600 s, expected × 500 ms)))`, else `config` unchanged; named constants for 500 ms per token and the 600 s ceiling. Both clients post chat requests with the returned config, so the existing timeout mapping names the effective timeout in `details`. No lower clamp exists: the configured timeout is the floor.
  - **Test first:** `RequestTimeoutsTest` as a `@CsvSource` of configured seconds, expected tokens → effective seconds: `180,40`→`180`, `180,400`→`200`, `180,1200`→`600`, `180,2000`→`600`, `30,40`→`30`, `30,400`→`200`, `900,2000`→`900`; a null expectation with `180` configured returns `180`. WireMock delay tests, **both dialects** (`/api/chat` and `/v1/chat/completions`), with a configured request timeout of 1 s and a stub delayed 1.5 s: a request expecting 400 tokens gets its reply (the scaled 200 s replaced the configured 1 s); a request expecting 1 token keeps the 1 s floor and fails with `ErrorCode.timeout` whose details contain `timeoutMs=1000`; a request stating no expectation fails the same way. In `GatedChatModelTest` (the dialect `chat_structuredOutputRejected_retriesOnceWithoutResponseFormat` uses), a request expecting 400 tokens whose format is rejected at once and whose retry is answered after 1.5 s, with a configured timeout of 1 s, gets its reply — the downgraded request kept its expected output.
  - **Log:** DEBUG the expected output tokens, the configured and the effective timeout, and which branch (scaled above the floor, floor kept, or no expectation) decided it.
  - **Done when:** `./gradlew :llm:check` is green.

The tab shows "reachable · 41 ms", "3 models", "inference · 1.2 s" (owner decision D-1); without measurement those badges have nothing to say, and without the shorter policies "Test connection" would have to call a model it may not have. → `:api`, `:llm`

The existing tests pass unchanged.

## 4. ∥ Inspection — after 1.1 and 1.4

- [ ] 4.1 Read the language evidence each format's metadata gives — the EPUB package's first `dc:language` against the language declared on the root element by more than half of the content documents that declare one, the FB2 `title-info/lang`, the Markdown frontmatter `lang` — normalize it, derive the match / mismatch / unrecognized / absent verdict, fill `Document.detectedSourceLang` with the EPUB content majority, and record on every EPUB and FB2 segment the language its block declares, as written. The import screen's language-mismatch warning and the Book Brief's preselected source both come from the evidence, and the run's foreign-passage rule needs each block's own declaration to compare with the source language the person settles on the brief — a comparison made at parse time against the package's language marked every paragraph of a Ukrainian book whose template said English as foreign (ADR-0037). → `:document`, `:api`

  - **Read:** `specs/document-round-trip/spec.md` "Report the language evidence a book's metadata gives" (every scenario), "Record the language a block declares on its segment" (every scenario); `specs/book-import/spec.md` "Warn when an EPUB's own language declarations disagree", "Warn when a book declares a language the application does not recognize"; `design.md` D11 (the declared block language — a declaration, never a comparison; `TreeNode` attribute read here); ADR-0037 (as amended); backlog decision debt D17 and D18 (`docs/implementation_plan/CHANGE_BACKLOG.md#decision-debt`); `modules/document/src/main/java/ua/bookloom/document/epub/{EpubReader,OpfParser,ParsedOpf}.java` (`EpubReader.read` passes `null` as `detectedSourceLang` today); `modules/document/src/main/java/ua/bookloom/document/fb2/Fb2Metadata.java` (`declaredLang`); `modules/document/src/main/java/ua/bookloom/document/md/Frontmatter.java` (`declaredLang`); `modules/document/src/main/java/ua/bookloom/document/model/{TreeNode,Jdom2TreeNode,JsoupTreeNode,BlockSegmentWalker}.java` (`TreeNode` has no attribute API; `BlockSegmentWalker.walk` sees only `TreeNode`); `modules/api/src/main/java/ua/bookloom/api/document/Segment.java` (only its canonical constructor today; 27 files construct it); `modules/document/src/test/java/ua/bookloom/document/epub/EpubZipBuilder.java` (fixture builder).
  - **Change:** `:document` `model.TreeNode` gains attribute **read** — `@Nullable String attribute(String qualifiedName)`, answering `xml:lang` and `lang` as written (JDOM2 reads `xml:lang` from the XML namespace) — implemented by `Jdom2TreeNode` and `JsoupTreeNode`. New package `ua.bookloom.document.inspect` (`package-info.java` with `@NullMarked` and a purpose Javadoc) with `LanguageEvidenceReader` (public, so the per-format inspections of task 4.2 call it) computing `LanguageEvidence` from a declared raw code and a list of content-document root declarations: normalize both sides, majority = the language declared by more than half of the documents that declare one, verdict per the requirement (a missing package language with a content majority is `ABSENT` with that majority). For EPUB the root declarations are read from each spine document's root start tag only (`xml:lang`, else `lang`), never the whole body, so inspection stays cheap. `EpubReader.read` fills `detectedSourceLang` with the normalized majority. `opens ua.bookloom.document.inspect to com.google.guice;` in `module-info.java`. `:api` `Segment` gains `@Nullable String declaredLanguage` — the `xml:lang` (else `lang`) value, as written, of the nearest element that encloses the segment's text and lies below the document's root element, the block itself included, or null — with the existing component list kept as a second constructor (declared language null), so no caller changes; `BlockSegmentWalker` tracks it while walking EPUB and FB2 trees and passes it into each segment; Markdown and TXT segments carry none. Parsing never compares it with any language and marks nothing as foreign.

- [ ] 4.2 Implement `BookInspector.inspect(Path)` as the one public façade `inspect.BookInspectorService` over four per-format inspections, answering readable, DRM-protected with the encryption scheme named from `META-INF/encryption.xml` where it can be identified — or `ZIP encryption` for a zipped FB2 whose entry is encrypted — or unsupported with the file type detected from the extension and the leading bytes, together with the format, its version and the language evidence, and record in the EPUB parser the manifest ids and properties, the package version, the cover meta, the guide and the spine's `toc` that inspection needs — without changing what `DocumentPort.open` returns for any file. The import screen must show "Adobe ADEPT — Import blocked" and "PDF — not supported" as distinct states, and both used to arrive as the same validation error told apart only by message text (ADR-0039); the command line's exit codes must not move; and the parser classes inspection needs are package-private, so the inspection of each format has to live inside that format's package. → `:document`
  - **Depends on:** task 4.1 (the `inspect` package and `LanguageEvidenceReader`), task 1.4 (the contracts).
  - **Excludes:** cover, structure and statistics (tasks 4.3–4.5); the Guice binding of `BookInspector` (task 4.5); the screen (task 12.1).
  - **Read:** `specs/document-round-trip/spec.md` "Inspect a book before opening it" (every scenario, "A password-protected zipped FB2 names its zip encryption" included); `specs/book-import/spec.md` "Block a DRM-protected book and name its encryption", "Refuse a file of an unsupported type and name what it is", "Open a book and report what was found" (the damaged-book scenario: the reason comes from opening); `design.md` D11 (the per-format inspections behind one façade, the magic bytes, the parser additions); ADR-0026 (adjudication by encrypted resource), ADR-0039; `modules/document/src/main/java/ua/bookloom/document/epub/DrmAdjudicator.java` (`adjudicate` throws `DrmRefusedException`; `refuseUnlessFont`, `encryptedResourcePaths`); `modules/document/src/main/java/ua/bookloom/document/FormatResolver.java` (package-private in the exported package; `resolve`, `startsWithZipMagic`, `looksLikeFictionBook`, `readProlog`); `modules/document/src/main/java/ua/bookloom/document/epub/{ContainerReader,OpfParser,ParsedOpf,ManifestItem,OpenEpubRegistry}.java` (`ManifestItem(href, mediaType)` drops the id and `properties`); `modules/document/src/main/java/ua/bookloom/document/model/ZipEncryption.java` (reads the zip central directory's encryption flag; `Fb2Reader` already uses it); `modules/document/src/main/java/module-info.java` (the format packages are already opened to Guice; `ua.bookloom.document.model` is not exported); `modules/document/src/test/java/ua/bookloom/document/fixture/RefusalFixtures.java`, `modules/document/src/test/java/ua/bookloom/document/golden/EpubRefusalFixturesTest.java`, `modules/document/src/test/java/ua/bookloom/document/epub/{DrmAdjudicatorTest,OpfParserTest}.java`.
  - **Change:** `inspect.FormatInspection` (interface: `inspect(Path) → BookInspection` for a file of its format, and the profile parts tasks 4.3–4.5 add) implemented by `epub.EpubInspection`, `fb2.Fb2Inspection`, `md.MarkdownInspection` and `txt.TxtInspection` — each public, `@RequiredArgsConstructor(onConstructor_ = {@Inject})`, inside its non-exported format package, so `OpfParser`, `ParsedEpub`, `ParsedFb2`, `Frontmatter` and the rest stay package-private — and `inspect.BookInspectorService` (`implements BookInspector`) choosing the inspection by format. `FormatResolver` moves to `ua.bookloom.document.model` as a public class with a public `resolve`, which `DocumentService` keeps calling (that package is not exported, so nothing outside `:document` sees it), and gains a non-throwing `probe(Path) → Optional<BookFormat>` the façade uses. The façade: a supported format → its inspection; otherwise `UNSUPPORTED` with `PDF` (bytes begin `%PDF-`), `DOCX` (a ZIP holding `[Content_Types].xml`), `MOBI` (`BOOKMOBI` at byte 60) or `Unknown`. `DrmAdjudicator` gains a non-throwing query answering whether content (not font-obfuscated fonts) is encrypted and which scheme: `Adobe ADEPT` when an `EncryptedData`'s `KeyInfo` is in the `http://ns.adobe.com/adept` namespace or `META-INF/rights.xml` is in it, `Readium LCP` when `META-INF/license.lcpl` exists, `Apple FairPlay` when `META-INF/sinf.xml` exists, otherwise none; `adjudicate` is rebuilt on it and still throws for `open`. `EpubInspection`: supported and not encrypted → `READABLE` with `EPUB ` + the package `version` (e.g. `EPUB 2.0`); encrypted → `DRM_PROTECTED` with the scheme or null; a container that cannot be read (a truncated ZIP) → `READABLE` with no version, so `open` supplies the reason. `Fb2Inspection`: `FB2`; a `.fb2.zip` whose entry carries the zip encryption flag (`model.ZipEncryption`) → `DRM_PROTECTED` with the scheme `ZIP encryption`. `MarkdownInspection` → `Markdown`, `TxtInspection` → `TXT`. Each fills its `LanguageEvidence` through `LanguageEvidenceReader`. Parser additions in `epub`: `ManifestItem(String id, String href, String mediaType, Set<String> properties)`; `ParsedOpf` records the package `version`, the `<meta name="cover">` content, the guide references (type and href) and the spine's `toc` attribute — read by tasks 4.3 and 4.4. Boundary `try/catch` → `internal`. `DocumentService.open` is not changed.
  - **Test first:** `BookInspectorServiceInspectTest` in `modules/document/src/test/java/ua/bookloom/document/inspect/` (created by this task), files in `@TempDir`: an EPUB with `version="2.0"` → `READABLE`, `EPUB 2.0`; a `version="3.0"` EPUB → `EPUB 3.0`; an EPUB whose `encryption.xml` encrypts its content documents with an ADEPT `KeyInfo` → `DRM_PROTECTED`, scheme `Adobe ADEPT`, and `DocumentService.open` on it → `ErrorCode.validation`; content encrypted with an unrecognized algorithm and no marker → `DRM_PROTECTED`, scheme null; only fonts under the IDPF obfuscation algorithm → `READABLE`; `Kobzar.fb2.zip` whose `Kobzar.fb2` entry carries the zip encryption flag → `DRM_PROTECTED`, scheme `ZIP encryption`, and `open` → `validation`; `book.pdf` beginning `%PDF-1.7` → `UNSUPPORTED`, `PDF`, and `open` → `validation`; `report.docx` (a ZIP holding `[Content_Types].xml` and `word/document.xml`) → `DOCX`; `mystery.bin` of 2,048 bytes from `new Random(42)` → `Unknown`; `broken.epub` truncated to half its length → `READABLE`, version null, and `open` → `validation`; `Forrest_Gump.fb2` fixture → `FB2`. `OpfParserTest`: a manifest item `id="nav" href="nav.xhtml" properties="nav scripted"` keeps its id and both properties; `version`, `<meta name="cover" content="cover-img"/>`, a guide `type="cover"` reference and `spine toc="ncx"` are recorded. `DrmAdjudicatorTest` and `EpubRefusalFixturesTest` pass unchanged.
  - **Log:** INFO one line per inspection (file name, verdict, format, version, detected type, scheme present); DEBUG each branch (magic bytes matched, encryption entry found, font-only encryption, zip encryption flag set, container unreadable); never a file's contents.
  - **Done when:** `./gradlew :document:check` is green and `./gradlew :app:test --tests '*TranslateCommand*'` passes unchanged.

A person recognises a book by its chapter names, the import card counts its chapters as the top-level nodes, and the structure screen's counts must add up to the book's total even when several table-of-contents entries point into one file or text sits outside every chapter. → `:document`
  - **Depends on:** task 4.2 (the per-format inspections, the manifest `properties` and the spine's `toc`).

  - **Change:** `FormatInspection` gains `structure(Document) → List<StructureNode>`. `epub.NavigationParser` (package-private, created here): finds the navigation document by the manifest's `nav` property and parses its `nav epub:type="toc"` `ol/li/a` entries with jsoup, and finds the NCX by the spine's `toc` attribute (else its media type `application/x-dtbncx+xml`) and parses its `navMap/navPoint` entries with JDOM2; each entry carries its label, its href resolved against the navigation file's directory with the fragment split off, its children, its 1-based entry path (`1.3.2`) and, for the NCX, its `navPoint` id. `EpubInspection.structure`: the navigation document's entries, else the NCX's, mapped onto spine units; the first entry into a unit carries its body-segment count (descendants included), a later entry into the same unit a null count; a spine unit no entry reaches is a top-level node at its spine position, titled by its first `HEADING` segment's text with placeholder tokens removed, else by its href's file name. `Fb2Inspection.structure`: each body's nested `section` elements titled by their `title` text; content of the main body outside every section is an untitled top-level node ahead of the sections; the notes body is a top-level node titled by its own `title`, else its `name` attribute. `MarkdownInspection.structure`: headings nested by level, each node counting the segments from its heading to the next heading of the same or higher level; text before the first heading is an untitled top-level node ahead of them. `TxtInspection.structure`: one node for the single unit. An untitled node has the empty title. The top-level counts sum to the body segment count in every format.

  - **Log:** DEBUG the navigation source used (nav, NCX, none), the entry count, entries whose target matched no spine unit, units titled by fallback (heading or file name), and the untitled leading and notes nodes added.

  - **Log:** INFO one line per profile (document id, node count, segment and word counts, cover present); DEBUG the counters' inputs and each fallback; never book text above TRACE.
