- [ ] 4.6 Write the target language everywhere a reading system looks for it on EPUB export — the OPF `dcterms:language` meta, the `<package>` element's `xml:lang`, and `xml:lang`/`lang` on each XHTML content document's `<html>` and `<body>` — wherever it carried the run's source language (compared after normalization), passing that source language to the writer through a new `DocumentPort.write` overload and falling back to the package's declared language when none is passed; append a missing `dc:language` with its `dc:` prefix, and reach a `dc:language` nested in the legacy `dc-metadata` wrapper. Reading systems choose hyphenation, fonts and the text-to-speech voice from these attributes; one surveyed book kept `xml:lang="en"` on 116 chapters after translation into Ukrainian (`docs/next_features.md` §1); and a book whose package says English while its chapters say Ukrainian is translated from Ukrainian, so only the run's source language can say which attributes follow. → `:document`, `:api`
  - **Depends on:** task 1.1 (`LanguageTags.normalize` for the comparison). It edits `EpubWriter`, which group 5 then continues to edit, so it lands before group 5.
  - **Excludes:** the OPF's declaration and line ends (task 5.1), attribute escaping (task 5.2), the XHTML prolog (task 5.3); the navigation document outside the spine, which task 6.3 re-serializes under this same rule; passing the brief's source language from the export job (task 10.8). A `<div lang>` or any deeper element keeps its language — a stated limit, not a defect.
  - **Read:** `specs/document-round-trip/spec.md` "Set the target language by replacing the first dc:language" (every scenario, "The run's source language decides which attributes follow", "Without a source language the package's declaration is used" and "A language set inside the body is kept" included); `design.md` D13 (the additive overload) and D14 §1; `docs/next_features.md` §1 (reproduction on `pg2760-images.epub` and *Building Microservices*); `modules/api/src/main/java/ua/bookloom/api/document/DocumentPort.java`; `modules/document/src/main/java/ua/bookloom/document/DocumentService.java` (`write`); `modules/document/src/main/java/ua/bookloom/document/epub/EpubWriter.java` (`setTargetLanguage`, which appends `new Element("language", DC_NS)` directly under `metadata` and only replaces the first direct child); the `DocumentPort` fakes that must keep compiling: `modules/ui/src/test/java/ua/bookloom/ui/ScriptedDocumentPort.java`, `modules/pipeline/src/test/java/ua/bookloom/pipeline/BookExporterTestSupport.java` (`ForwardingDocumentPort`), the anonymous ports in `modules/pipeline/src/test/java/ua/bookloom/pipeline/{SegmentTranslatorTest,StructuredReplySegmentTranslatorTest}.java`; `modules/document/src/test/java/ua/bookloom/document/epub/EpubWriterTest.java`; `modules/document/src/test/java/ua/bookloom/document/golden/{EpubGoldenRoundTripTest,EpubCanonicalAssert,GoldenComparisonMetaTest}.java` (the comparator's allowance for the intentional language metadata).
  - **Change:** `:api` `DocumentPort` gains `write(Document document, Path destination, @Nullable String sourceLanguage, String targetLanguage)` as a `default` method that calls the three-argument `write` (so every fake keeps compiling and ignores the source language); `DocumentService` overrides it with the real write and its three-argument `write` delegates with a null source language. The FB2, Markdown and TXT writers ignore the source language. `EpubWriter.setTargetLanguage` looks for the first `dc:language` under `metadata`, else inside `metadata/dc-metadata`, replacing it or appending one with the `dc` prefix; it rewrites `meta[@property="dcterms:language"]` and `package/@xml:lang` when their normalized value equals the source language — the one passed, else the package's first declared `dc:language` — and the spine serialization rewrites `xml:lang` and `lang` on `html` and `body` under the same condition, leaving any other language and any other element untouched. `EpubCanonicalAssert`'s language allowance covers exactly these attributes, so any other attribute change is still a difference.
  - **Test first:** `EpubWriterTest` (target `uk`): `<dc:language>en</dc:language><dc:language>la</dc:language>` → `uk` then `la`; no `dc:language` → exactly one, serialized as `<dc:language>uk</dc:language>` in the Dublin Core namespace; `<dc-metadata><dc:language>en-GB</dc:language></dc-metadata>` → the nested element reads `uk` and no second one exists; `<meta property="dcterms:language">en</meta>` → `uk`; `<package xml:lang="en">` → `xml:lang="uk"`; a book declaring `en-US` whose chapter has `<html xml:lang="en" lang="en">` and `<body lang="en">`, written from source `en` → `uk` on all three; a chapter `<html xml:lang="la">` stays `la`; a package `xml:lang="en"` with a chapter `<html xml:lang="uk">` written from source `uk` to target `de` → the chapter reads `de` and the package still `en`; a package declaring `en` with a chapter `<html lang="en">` written with no source language → `uk`; `<body lang="en"><div lang="en"><p>Text.</p></div></body>` from `en` → the body reads `uk` and the `div` still `en`. `DocumentServiceTest`: the three-argument `write` behaves as the four-argument one with a null source language. Golden: `./gradlew :document:test --tests 'ua.bookloom.document.golden.*'` stays green (canonical-equal apart from the language metadata), and a new `GoldenComparisonMetaTest` case proves a changed `class` attribute on `<html>` is still reported.
  - **Log:** DEBUG the passed and declared source languages and the target, each language slot found and whether it was rewritten or kept (with its value), and whether a `dc:language` was appended or found nested.
  - **Done when:** `./gradlew :document:test --tests 'ua.bookloom.document.golden.*'` and, closing the group, `./gradlew build :app:archTest` are green.

## 5. Write fidelity and splitting — after group 4 (the same reader and writer files)

- [ ] 5.1 Write the EPUB package document with line-feed line ends only, and with an XML declaration only when the source package document had one, keeping the encoding it named. The OPF gained a declaration it never had and carriage returns it never had, and the golden comparison could not see it because it re-serializes both sides the same way (`docs/next_features.md` §2) — a file that changes where nothing was translated is what "canonical-equal" is meant to rule out. → `:document`
  - **Depends on:** task 4.6 (the last group-4 edit of `EpubWriter`).
  - **Excludes:** the XHTML content documents (tasks 5.2, 5.3); the navigation document and the NCX, which task 6.3 re-serializes under this same rule.
  - **Read:** `specs/document-round-trip/spec.md` "Keep the package document's line ends and declaration"; `docs/next_features.md` §2; `modules/document/src/main/java/ua/bookloom/document/epub/EpubWriter.java` (`serializeOpf`: `new XMLOutputter(Format.getRawFormat())`); `modules/document/src/main/java/ua/bookloom/document/epub/{OpfParser,ParsedOpf}.java`; `modules/document/src/main/java/ua/bookloom/document/model/Jdom2TreeNode.java` (the LF separator pinned there); `modules/document/src/test/java/ua/bookloom/document/golden/EpubCanonicalAssert.java` (why the comparator cannot see it).
  - **Change:** `OpfParser` records in `ParsedOpf` whether the source bytes began with an XML declaration and the encoding it named; `serializeOpf` uses a raw format with `LineSeparator.UNIX`, omits the declaration when the source had none, and otherwise writes it with the recorded encoding (and encodes the bytes in it). The rule is written as one helper the NCX serialization of task 6.3 calls too.
  - **Test first:** `EpubWriterTest` asserting the written `OEBPS/content.opf` bytes directly (the comparator cannot): a source package with no declaration and only LF, written with zero edits, has no `<?xml` prefix and no `0x0D` byte; a source beginning `<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?>` begins with a declaration naming `UTF-8`. The per-format golden stays green.
  - **Log:** DEBUG whether the source had a declaration, its encoding, and the choice made when writing.
  - **Done when:** `./gradlew :document:test --tests 'ua.bookloom.document.golden.*'` and `./gradlew :document:check` are green.

- [ ] 5.2 Keep a line feed, carriage return or tab inside an XHTML attribute value as a character reference when writing, through a serialization post-pass, and make the canonical comparator normalize attribute values the way an XML reader does, so the golden test can see this class of change. An XML reader turns a raw line feed in an attribute into a space, so writing `&#10;` back as a raw line feed silently changes the value — one surveyed book loses a line break in a bookmark label (`docs/next_features.md` §3) — and jsoup cannot keep the reference through its tree, so the only place to put it back is the serialized output. → `:document`
  - **Depends on:** task 5.1.
  - **Excludes:** translating attribute values (group 6).
  - **Read:** `specs/document-round-trip/spec.md` "Keep line-feed references in attribute values"; `design.md` D14 §3 (the post-pass); `docs/next_features.md` §3 (the `data-pdf-bookmark` evidence); `modules/document/src/main/java/ua/bookloom/document/epub/{XhtmlParser,EpubWriter,PreformattedLineFeedRestorer}.java` (jsoup with `syntax(xml)`); `modules/document/src/test/java/ua/bookloom/document/golden/{EpubCanonicalAssert,GoldenComparisonMetaTest}.java`.
  - **Change:** content-document serialization, on the serialization copy only (never the registry-held tree): replace each `\n`, `\r` and `\t` inside an attribute value with the private-use sentinels U+E00A, U+E00D and U+E009, serialize, then replace each sentinel in the output string with `&#10;`, `&#13;` and `&#9;`; a document whose source already contains one of those sentinel code points anywhere skips the post-pass, with one DEBUG line, and is written as jsoup writes it today. `EpubCanonicalAssert` reads both sides with XML attribute-value normalization, so a raw line feed and a reference no longer compare equal.
  - **Test first:** `EpubWriterTest`: an element `data-pdf-bookmark="Communication: &#10;So Many Choices"` written with zero edits carries a line-feed reference in the output bytes, and re-reading the output with an XML parser yields a value containing `\n`, not a space; a document that already contains U+E00A in its text skips the post-pass and writes that attribute's line feed raw. `GoldenComparisonMetaTest`: an output that writes that attribute with a raw line feed is reported as a difference (red before the comparator change, the proof the golden now sees it). The golden suite stays green.
  - **Log:** DEBUG the count of attribute values escaped per document, and a skipped post-pass with its reason (no values; attribute text is book text and goes to TRACE only).
  - **Done when:** `./gradlew :document:test --tests 'ua.bookloom.document.golden.*'` and `./gradlew :document:check` are green.

- [ ] 5.3 Keep an XHTML content document's prolog — its XML declaration and DOCTYPE — verbatim and write it ahead of the serialized document, instead of letting jsoup turn the declaration into a `<!--?xml … ?-->` comment and normalize the DOCTYPE's quotes and line breaks. For a UTF-8 chapter the comment is harmless; for a chapter in any other encoding the reader no longer learns the encoding and decodes it wrongly (`docs/next_features.md` §4). → `:document`
  - **Depends on:** task 5.2.
  - **Excludes:** transcoding (an EPUB never needs it — jsoup writes numeric character references, ADR-0029 as amended; TXT and Markdown are task 5.5); the navigation document, which task 6.3 writes under this same rule.
  - **Read:** `specs/document-round-trip/spec.md` "Keep an XHTML document's XML declaration as a declaration" ("The declaration stays a declaration", "The DOCTYPE is kept as written"); `docs/next_features.md` §4; `docs/implementation_plan/notes-corpus-verification.md` (the first observation); `modules/document/src/main/java/ua/bookloom/document/epub/{XhtmlParser,EpubWriter,ParsedEpub}.java` (`serializeContentDocument`).
  - **Change:** at parse time record, per spine document, the source prolog bytes (everything before the root element's start tag); at write time emit that prolog followed by the serialized root element, with jsoup's own declaration and DOCTYPE suppressed.
  - **Test first:** `EpubWriterTest`: a content document beginning `<?xml version='1.0' encoding='utf-8'?>` written with zero edits begins with exactly that and contains no `<!--?xml`; a DOCTYPE spanning two lines with single-quoted identifiers is carried with the same quotes and line break; a document with no prolog gains none. The golden suite (including `pg2760`-style fixtures in `EpubFixtures`) stays green.
  - **Log:** DEBUG per document whether a prolog was recorded and its length in bytes.
  - **Done when:** `./gradlew :document:test --tests 'ua.bookloom.document.golden.*'` and `./gradlew :document:check` are green.

- [ ] 5.4 Record an FB2 book's byte-order mark and write it back, and add none to a book that had none. The encoding rules already say a mark is recorded and re-emitted exactly as found; plain text does that and FB2 dropped it, and some readers rely on the mark to choose the encoding (`docs/next_features.md` §5). → `:document`
  - **Depends on:** task 5.3 (group 5 is sequential).
  - **Excludes:** any other FB2 encoding policy (backlog D11, a non-goal).
  - **Read:** `specs/document-round-trip/spec.md` "Write an FB2 byte-order mark back" (both scenarios); `docs/specification/01_Product/03_DOCUMENT_FORMATS.md#encoding-and-bom`; `docs/next_features.md` §5; `modules/document/src/main/java/ua/bookloom/document/fb2/{Fb2Reader,ParsedFb2,Fb2Writer,Fb2Encoding}.java` (the reader already passes `resolution.hasBom()` into `Document.hasBom`; the writer ignores it); `modules/document/src/main/java/ua/bookloom/document/txt/{TxtReader,TxtWriter}.java` (the pattern to copy).
  - **Change:** `ParsedFb2` records the mark's bytes (or none); `Fb2Writer` writes them first.
  - **Test first:** `Fb2WriterTest`: `Bom.fb2` — the `Forrest_Gump` fixture prefixed with `EF BB BF`, in `@TempDir` — written with zero edits begins with `EF BB BF`; the unprefixed fixture begins with `<?xml`. `FormatGoldenRoundTripTest` gains the prefixed fixture and stays canonical-equal.
  - **Log:** DEBUG whether a mark was recorded and written.
  - **Done when:** `./gradlew :document:test --tests 'ua.bookloom.document.golden.*'` and `./gradlew :document:check` are green.

- [ ] 5.5 Write a TXT or Markdown export whose translation the encoding resolved at import cannot hold as UTF-8 — the whole document, with a byte-order mark exactly when the source had one and UTF-8 recorded as the document's charset — instead of refusing it (TXT) or writing `?` (Markdown). The refusal threw away a finished translation over a code-page detail the person cannot change — a `windows-1252` book translated into Ukrainian has almost no representable character — and the `?` destroyed the text silently; switching the whole file keeps every character, as FB2 always has (owner decision D-4, ADR-0029 as amended, `docs/next_features.md` §6 and §10). → `:document`
  - **Depends on:** task 5.4.
  - **Excludes:** EPUB, which writes a numeric character reference and never switches (task 5.6 pins it); FB2, whose switch is unchanged; the zero-edit goldens, which stay as they are.
  - **Read:** `specs/document-round-trip/spec.md` "Write a TXT or Markdown export as UTF-8 when its encoding cannot hold the translation" (every scenario), "Reassemble plain text by splicing target spans into the original bytes", "Reassemble Markdown by splicing translated spans into the original bytes", "Pass a golden round-trip test for TXT", and the removed "Refuse a plain-text export the source encoding cannot represent" with its migration note (no refusal and no refusal title exist any more); ADR-0029 (as amended: implemented for TXT and Markdown); `design.md` D14 §6; `docs/next_features.md` §6, §10; `modules/document/src/main/java/ua/bookloom/document/txt/{TxtWriter,TxtReader}.java` (`encode` throws `CorruptContainerException` today); `modules/document/src/main/java/ua/bookloom/document/md/{MarkdownWriter,MarkdownReader,ParsedMarkdown}.java` (`targetInner.getBytes(parsed.charset())` writes `?`); `modules/document/src/main/java/ua/bookloom/document/detect/{CharsetLadder,ForeignWordCoherence}.java` (how a re-open resolves the charset; `ForeignWordCoherence.prefer` never overrides a multi-byte winner); `modules/document/src/main/java/ua/bookloom/document/fb2/Fb2Writer.java` (`serialize`, the switch this follows); `modules/document/src/main/java/ua/bookloom/document/DocumentService.java` (`write`); `modules/document/src/test/java/ua/bookloom/document/txt/TxtRoundTripTest.java` (`write_unrepresentableTargetCharacter_failsAndWritesNoFile`, which this task rewrites); `modules/document/src/test/java/ua/bookloom/document/golden/FormatGoldenRoundTripTest.java` (the FB2 encoding-switch case asserted against a re-parsed canonical tree — the carve-out this extends).
  - **Change:** `TxtWriter` and `MarkdownWriter` first encode the assembled output in the charset resolved at import with a `CharsetEncoder` set to `REPORT`; when any character is unrepresentable they decode the untouched source bytes with that charset, splice the targets into the decoded text, and encode the **whole** document as UTF-8, so no byte of the old code page survives; they write a UTF-8 byte-order mark exactly when the source had a mark (its presence, not its bytes) and set the registry's recorded charset (`ParsedMarkdown`, the TXT registry state) to UTF-8. Markdown never writes `?` again. `TxtWriter` no longer throws `CorruptContainerException` for an unrepresentable character, so `DocumentService.write` refuses no export for its encoding. No new exception type, no new error title.
  - **Test first:** `TxtRoundTripTest`: the rewritten case — a TXT read as `windows-1251`, with no byte-order mark, holding `Привіт.\n\nHello.\n`, written with the target `車.` for its second segment → the output decodes as UTF-8 to `Привіт.\n\n車.\n`, begins with no byte-order mark, holds no `?`, and re-opening it resolves `UTF-8`; `windows-1252` `Hello.\n` with the target `Привіт.` → the export succeeds and decodes as UTF-8 to `Привіт.\n`; `windows-1251` `One.\n\nTwo.\n` with the target `Два — три.` for its second segment → the output decodes as `windows-1251` to `One.\n\nДва — три.\n` and the bytes before the second paragraph are unchanged. `MarkdownRoundTripTest`: a Markdown file read as `ISO-8859-1` whose one segment's target contains `Ÿ` → the whole output decodes as UTF-8, contains `Ÿ`, and holds no `?` in its place. `FormatGoldenRoundTripTest`: an encoding-switched TXT fixture and an encoding-switched Markdown fixture are each asserted against the expected text re-parsed from the written file (TXT by decoded text, Markdown re-parse-equal) instead of the source's bytes; the zero-edit goldens are unchanged.
  - **Log:** INFO once per switched document (document id, format, source charset, `UTF-8`, whether a mark was written); DEBUG the reporting encoder's outcome; never the character or the text above TRACE.
  - **Done when:** `./gradlew :document:test --tests 'ua.bookloom.document.golden.*'` and `./gradlew :document:check` are green.

A swapped pair `⟦g1⟧OLD⟦g0⟧` passed the count-only check, EPUB silently "repaired" it and FB2 refused it at write time, failing a whole export with no segment named (`docs/next_features.md` §9, backlog D7 and D8); an emptied pair `⟦g0⟧⟦g1⟧OLD` loses the emphasis on the word, and a line break moved across a pair's edge changes how the written paragraph splits into runs, so the re-opened book has a different segment count and the export fails at its final check — checking here flags that one segment instead (ADR-0040 as amended); and the pair's own language is what lets the run keep a marked foreign passage verbatim (owner decision D-6). → `:document`, `:api`

A paragraph longer than the chunk budget must be translated in pieces that fit back together exactly — one lost or doubled space shows in the book — and a piece holding half a pair can never pass the gate (backlog D5, ADR-0040); read raw, ICU takes `⟦` followed by `g` for a sentence that continues, so sentences that begin or end with inline markup would never split. → `:document`

  - **Excludes:** deciding when to split and drafting the pieces (task 7.5); estimating tokens (task 7.3).
  - **Read:** `specs/document-round-trip/spec.md` "Split an oversized segment at sentence boundaries" (every scenario, "A sentence that starts with inline markup is split before its opening token" and "An opening token right after a sentence end starts the next piece" included); `specs/translation-pipeline/spec.md` "Split an oversized segment at sentence boundaries"; `design.md` D4 (the splitting steps and why the splitter sits in `:document`); ADR-0040; backlog D5; `modules/api/src/main/java/ua/bookloom/api/document/SentenceSplitter.java` (task 1.4); `modules/document/src/main/java/ua/bookloom/document/DocumentModule.java`; `modules/document/src/main/java/module-info.java` (ICU4J is already required).
  - **Change:** `split.IcuSentenceSplitter`: (1) replace every `⟦gN⟧` token of the masked text with U+FFFC (OBJECT REPLACEMENT CHARACTER), keeping an offset map; (2) run `com.ibm.icu.text.BreakIterator.getSentenceInstance(ULocale.forLanguageTag(languageTag))` over the neutralized text and map each boundary back to its offset in the masked text; (3) move each boundary forward past any closing or atomic tokens that immediately follow it and past the whitespace after them, leaving an opening token after the boundary; (4) drop a boundary at which a pair is still open, by pair depth over `segment.pairs()` — a token that is in no pair (a protected span of the pipeline included) is atomic; (5) cut the pieces, which concatenate exactly to the input, returning `Result.ok(List.of(masked))` when no boundary survives. Boundary `try/catch` → `internal`. `package-info.java` with `@NullMarked` and a purpose Javadoc; `opens ua.bookloom.document.split to com.google.guice;`; `DocumentModule` binds `SentenceSplitter` to it.

Two. Three.⟦g1⟧` → one piece; 6,000 characters of `word ` repeated with no terminal punctuation → one piece equal to the input; in every case `String.join("", pieces)` equals the input (asserted per row of one `@ParameterizedTest`). `DocumentModuleTest`: the injector returns a `SentenceSplitter`.

- [ ] 6.3 Produce the EPUB navigation document's link texts, the NCX labels that differ from them and every image's `alt` value as auxiliary segments, and write them back — carving the navigation document and the NCX out of "out-of-spine resources are verbatim" while keeping their structure, order, `playOrder` and link targets; writing an NCX label that reads exactly like a navigation label from that navigation segment's target; producing no navigation label for a navigation document that is itself in the spine; and giving an image inside a paragraph its translated `alt` whether or not the paragraph was translated, encoded exactly once. One surveyed book kept 494 table-of-contents labels in English beside translated chapter headings (`docs/next_features.md` §8); translating an identical NCX label a second time wastes a model call and lets a reviewer's edit reach only one of the two; and restoring a paragraph that holds an image would otherwise put the untranslated description back, or print `&amp;amp;` (DD-47, ADR-0041). → `:document`

ADR-0041; `design.md` D12 (the slot table rows, the NCX dedupe, "Restore by kind", "ALT write-back") and D14 §1, §2, §4; `docs/next_features.md` §8 (*Building Microservices*: `OEBPS/toc01.html` carries `properties="nav"` and is not in the spine); `modules/document/src/main/java/ua/bookloom/document/epub/{EpubReader,EpubWriter,XhtmlParser,ParsedEpub,NavigationParser}.java` (`NavigationParser` from task 4.4); `modules/document/src/main/java/ua/bookloom/document/model/{SkeletonAnchors,TreeMasker}.java` (`SkeletonAnchors.writeBlock` → `TreeNode.replaceChildren`, around `:127`, replace a node's children from the restored markup, re-parsing any inline `<img alt=…>`); `modules/document/src/main/java/ua/bookloom/document/DocumentService.java` (`unmask`, lines 198–205, escapes text as markup; `write`, line 273).
  - **Change:** the reader reuses task 4.4's `NavigationParser`. When the navigation document is **not** in the spine, each nav link's content becomes a `NAV_LABEL` markup slot `aux:nav:<entry path>` (e.g. `aux:nav:1.3.2`) anchored into the navigation tree; each NCX `navPoint`'s `navLabel/text` becomes a `NAV_LABEL` text slot `aux:ncx:<navPoint id>` only when its source text differs from every navigation-label segment's, and otherwise the slot table records it as written from that navigation segment. When the navigation document is in the spine it yields no `NAV_LABEL` (its links are body segments) and every NCX label is its own segment. Each non-empty `img@alt` in a spine document becomes an `ALT` attribute slot `aux:alt:<unit href>:<node path>` (`AttributeAnchor`). `DocumentService.unmask` restores an `ALT` segment to plain text — every placeholder substituted back, no markup escaping. The writer re-serializes the navigation document when one of its labels has a target or its `html`/`body` language attribute equals the source language (task 4.6's rule), and the NCX when one of its labels has a target, each following task 5.1's line-end and declaration rule and, for the navigation document, task 5.3's prolog rule; otherwise it copies their bytes. An NCX label recorded as written from a navigation segment takes that segment's effective target, so a reviewer's edit reaches both. `ALT` write-back: when the body run that holds the image has a target, the writer finds the image's exact source fragment inside that run's restored markup — the k-th occurrence of identical markup matching the k-th image — and substitutes the same fragment with the translated alt, attribute-encoded, before the run is written, and does not also set the tree attribute (the run's re-parse would overwrite it); when the run has no target, or the image is in no run, the writer sets the attribute on the tree through `TreeNode.setAttribute` as plain text.
  - **Test first:** `EpubReaderTest`: a navigation document outside the spine with 11 entries, an NCX whose 11 labels read exactly like them and 7 images with non-empty `alt` → 11 `NAV_LABEL` and 7 `ALT` segments; a navigation document labelling an entry `Chapter 1` and an NCX labelling it `Chapter I` → two `NAV_LABEL` segments; `nav.xhtml` in the spine with 5 entries → no `NAV_LABEL`, and the 5 link texts are segments of the `nav.xhtml` body unit; `OEBPS/toc01.html` outside the spine with 3 entries → 3 labels with ids `aux:nav:1`, `aux:nav:2`, `aux:nav:3`. `EpubWriterTest`: an NCX `navPoint playOrder="4"` labelled `Chapter Three` pointing at `c03.xhtml#start`, with no navigation document, and the target `Розділ третій` keeps `playOrder="4"` and the link and reads `Розділ третій`; a navigation document and an NCX both labelling a chapter `The Storm`, with the one navigation segment targeted `Буря` → both read `Буря`, and with that segment's saved edit `Шторм` → both read `Шторм`; `<p>Before <img src="fig1.png" alt="Figure 1"/> after</p>` with the body target `До ⟦g0⟧ після` and the alt target `Рисунок 1` → `<p>До <img src="fig1.png" alt="Рисунок 1"/> після</p>`; with the body target `⟦g0⟧ До після` (the image moved) → the image still reads `alt="Рисунок 1"`; with no body target → `<p>Before <img src="fig1.png" alt="Рисунок 1"/> after</p>`; `<p><img src="dot.png" alt="Dot"/> and <img src="dot.png" alt="Dot"/></p>` with the body target `⟦g0⟧ і ⟦g1⟧` and the alt targets `Перша точка` and `Друга точка` → the first image carries `Перша точка` and the second `Друга точка`; the alt target `Том & Джеррі`, inside a translated and inside an untranslated paragraph → `alt="Том &amp; Джеррі"` and no `&amp;amp;` anywhere; `<a href="#n1" title="Footnote one">1</a>` fully translated keeps `href="#n1"` and `title="Footnote one"`; a navigation document whose `<html>` carries `xml:lang="en"`, exported from `en` to `uk` → `xml:lang="uk"`, its prolog kept byte for byte; a rewritten NCX that had no declaration and only LF gains neither. Golden suite green (zero edits: the navigation document and the NCX are copied byte for byte unless their root language follows, which the canonical comparator's language allowance covers).
  - **Log:** DEBUG counts of navigation, NCX and alt segments, NCX labels written from a navigation segment (by id), whether each navigation resource was re-serialized or copied and why, and for each alt write whether it was substituted in a run or set on the tree; TRACE the texts.
  - **Done when:** `./gradlew :document:test --tests 'ua.bookloom.document.golden.*'` and `./gradlew build` are green.

Keys are field names a program reads, and dates, flags, counts and addresses are data a site generator parses, so translating any of them breaks the file (owner decision D-2); a translation such as `Книга: Частина 1` or `1984` written unquoted becomes a mapping or a number to a YAML reader; a file that still says `lang: en` after translation claims to be English; and eleven images in one surveyed Markdown document kept their English alt text (`docs/next_features.md` §8). → `:document`
  - **Depends on:** task 6.4.
  - **Excludes:** whether a run translates frontmatter values — off by default (the brief, task 1.2; the run's switches, task 10.2).
  - **Read:** `specs/document-round-trip/spec.md` "Segment only the text values of Markdown frontmatter, never its keys" (every scenario), "Quote a translated frontmatter value so it still reads as text" (every scenario), "Declare no language metadata in formats that carry none" (every scenario), "Present a buffer-shaped segment's masked form as its own source text" ("A quoted frontmatter value is read inside its quotes", "An escaped bracket in an image description is read as a bracket"), "Produce a book's auxiliary text as translatable segments" ("Only a frontmatter text value is a segment"), "Write auxiliary translations back into their own slots" ("A frontmatter value is replaced inside the frontmatter", "A Markdown image description escapes its brackets"), "Restore an image's alternative text and a frontmatter value as plain text" ("A frontmatter value gains no Markdown backslashes"), "Give every unit a stable identity appropriate to its format" ("A frontmatter value is named by its key"); the removed "Preserve Markdown frontmatter verbatim without segmenting it" and its migration note; `design.md` D12 (the frontmatter classification, quoting and `lang` rules, "Restore by kind", the Markdown alt span); `modules/document/src/main/java/ua/bookloom/document/md/{Frontmatter,MarkdownReader,MarkdownWriter,MarkdownMasker,MarkdownEscaper,MarkdownStructureCheck}.java`; `modules/document/src/main/java/ua/bookloom/document/DocumentService.java` (`unmask` always runs `MarkdownEscaper` and `MarkdownStructureCheck` for Markdown, lines 198–205 and 276–283); `modules/document/src/main/java/ua/bookloom/document/model/BufferReassembler.java` (replacements must not overlap); `modules/document/src/test/java/ua/bookloom/document/md/MarkdownRoundTripTest.java` (`read_frontmatter_yieldsNoSegmentForItsKeysOrValues` and `write_zeroEditRoundTrip_isByteIdentical` over `MarkdownFixtures.PRIMARY`, whose frontmatter holds `title: The Book` and `lang: en`).
  - **Change:** `Frontmatter` records, for each top-level line, the key, the value's byte span (inside its quotes when quoted, a trailing `# comment` excluded when unquoted) and its quote style, and classifies the value as text only when all hold: the key is neither `lang` nor `language`; it is a single-line scalar on a top-level `key: value` line (not indented, not a list item, not a flow collection opening `[` or `{`, not a block scalar opening `|` or `>`, not an anchor, alias or tag opening `&`, `*` or `!`); unquoted, it is not a null (`~`, `null` in any case, empty), a boolean (`true`, `false`, `yes`, `no`, `on`, `off`, `y`, `n` in any case), a number (hexadecimal, octal, `.inf` and `.nan` included) or an ISO date or date-time; it is not a URL or e-mail (`scheme://`, `www.`, `mailto:`, `name@host.domain`); and it holds a letter. `MarkdownReader` produces, only when the file opens with `---`, a `FRONTMATTER_VALUE` segment `aux:fm:<key>` per text value, whose source is the value inside its quotes with `\"` or `''` decoded, and an `ALT` segment `aux:alt:<file name>:img<k>` per image's alt span with its backslash escapes decoded — both `ByteSpanAnchor`s. `DocumentService.unmask` restores `FRONTMATTER_VALUE` and `ALT` to plain text: every placeholder substituted back, no Markdown escaping, no structure check. `MarkdownWriter` writes a value back inside its original quotes (`"` written `\"` inside double quotes, `'` written `''` inside single quotes), and wraps an unquoted translation in double quotes when it contains `: ` or ` #`, starts with one of `-`, `?`, `:`, `,`, `[`, `]`, `{`, `}`, `#`, `&`, `*`, `!`, `|`, `>`, `'`, `"`, `%`, `@` or a backtick, or would itself read as a null, boolean, number or date; it replaces an existing top-level `lang` value with the target tag in that value's quote style, changes nothing else in the frontmatter, never adds a `lang` key and leaves `language` alone; an alt target is written with `\`, `[` and `]` escaped — inside the paragraph's restored image token when the paragraph also has a body target (the spans would overlap), otherwise as its own replacement.
  - **Test first:** `MarkdownRoundTripTest`, with the existing tests rewritten to the new rules: `MarkdownFixtures.PRIMARY` (frontmatter `title: The Book`, `lang: en`) yields exactly one auxiliary segment, `aux:fm:title` holding `The Book`, and none holding `title`, `lang` or `en`; written with zero edits and target `uk`, its output equals the source except that `lang: en` reads `lang: uk`; `---\ntitle: The Book\nlang: en\ndate: 2024-05-01\ndraft: true\n---\n` → exactly one segment, `The Book`; the lines `tags: [war, peace]`, `homepage: https://example.com`, `pages: 1225`, `published: yes`, `summary: |` and `  A long story.` → no segment; `subtitle: "Part 2"` and `edition: "2"` → a segment `Part 2` and none holding `2` alone; `title: The Book # working title` → the segment holds `The Book`; a file beginning `# Title` with a later `---` line → no auxiliary segment and two body segments; the target `Книга` for `title: The Book` → output begins `---\ntitle: Книга\n---\n`; `Книга: Частина 1` → `title: "Книга: Частина 1"`; `1984` for `title: Nineteen Eighty-Four` → `title: "1984"`; `Книга "Перша"` for `title: "The Book"` → `title: "Книга \"Перша\""`; `Книга 'Перша'` for `title: 'The Book'` → `title: 'Книга ''Перша'''`; `Том & Джеррі *назавжди*` → `title: Том & Джеррі *назавжди*` with no backslash; `---\ntitle: The Book\n---\n` exported to `uk` → unchanged; `lang: "en-US"` → `lang: "uk"`; `language: en` with no `lang` → unchanged and no `lang` key added; `![Figure 1](fig1.png)` with the alt target `Рисунок 1` → `![Рисунок 1](fig1.png)`, with and without a body target on its paragraph; `Рисунок [1]` → `![Рисунок \[1\]](fig1.png)`; the source alt `Figure \[1\]` is read as `Figure [1]`. `FormatGoldenRoundTripTest` (Markdown) stays re-parse-equal.
  - **Log:** DEBUG the frontmatter keys seen (names are not book text), how many values were taken as text and why each other was not (the rule, not the value), the alt count, each quoting decision and whether a `lang` value was replaced; TRACE the values.
  - **Done when:** `./gradlew :document:test --tests 'ua.bookloom.document.golden.*'` and `./gradlew build` are green.

  - **Change:** `project.RoundTripCheck` used by `ProjectServiceImpl.roundTrip(projectId)`: open the project's source afresh, write it unedited into a fresh `Files.createTempDirectory` under the source's file name through the four-argument `DocumentPort.write`, passing the book's declared language as both source and target language so no language attribute or segment changes — or no source language and `und` as the target when the book declares none — re-open the copy, compare segment count, ids in order, and each segment's masked text and placeholder map → `structurePreserved`, filling `sourceSegments` and `copySegments` with the two body segment counts; compare the two profiles' resource ids → `idsPreserved` and `missingIds`; close both documents and delete the temporary directory in `finally`; any unexpected failure becomes `ErrorCode.internal` at the boundary.
  - **Test first:** `RoundTripCheckTest`: a generated EPUB with one image and one font reports structure preserved, ids preserved and no missing id; the same book through a hand-written `DocumentPort` decorator whose `write` then removes the image entry from the written archive reports ids not preserved with the image's id missing; a decorator whose write drops the second paragraph reports structure not preserved with `copySegments` one below `sourceSegments` (equal counts for the faithful book); an EPUB declaring `en` whose chapter carries `<html xml:lang="en">` is written with that declaration unchanged (the check passes source and target `en`); after each check the temporary directory no longer exists; `roundTrip("missing")` answers `ErrorCode.validation`. Integration tier.
  - **Log:** INFO the check's result: project id, structure preserved, ids preserved, missing-id count; DEBUG the temporary path, the languages passed to the writer and both segment counts; ERROR an unexpected failure once, with its cause.
  - **Done when:** `./gradlew :pipeline:test --tests 'ua.bookloom.pipeline.project.RoundTripCheckTest'` is green and, closing the group, `./gradlew build :app:archTest` is green.

## 8. Quality gates — after group 7

- [ ] 8.1 Add the five deterministic soft checks — target script, untranslated echo, repetition loop, length ratio against the pair's band, and glossary compliance over locked terms — each yielding a pass or fail and a margin between 0 and 1, marking a failure as blocking except a failed echo below the 20-code-point floor, with names kept by policy left out of the script and echo checks and both checks skipped for a foreign passage kept by policy. Without them a restored reply is accepted on its tokens alone, so a copied source, a Latin-letter "Ukrainian" answer or a decode loop goes straight into the book; and under owner decision D-3 a check that fails outright must block acceptance, while a short line such as `OK` or a name may read the same in both languages. → `:pipeline`
  - **Depends on:** task 1.1 (each language's `Script`, `LanguageTags.normalize`); task 1.2 (`QaFinding`, `Severity`, `ForeignPassagePolicy`, `NamePolicy`); task 4.1 (`Segment.declaredLanguage`, the block language the caller passes in); task 7.3 (`LengthBand`, `DisplayText`).
  - **Excludes:** the refusal gate, the confidence blend and the evaluator that reports a blocking failure — task 8.2; the placeholder and protected-span hard gates — tasks 5.8 and 9.3; feeding the segment's locked terms into the glossary check — task 9.3 (here the check takes a plain list); where the checks run in the pipeline — task 8.6.
  - **Read:** `specs/quality-gates/spec.md` "Check the target script for pairs whose languages use different scripts", "Fail a target that echoes its source", "Fail a target caught in a repetition loop", "Check the length ratio against the band of the language pair", "Measure glossary compliance over the locked terms in the segment", "Skip the echo and script checks for a marked foreign passage kept by policy", "Block acceptance when a soft check fails outright" (every scenario of each); design D8 (display texts, the soft-check table with each margin, the names removed under the keep-original policy, the echo floor, foreign keep), "Tuning constants" (the check rows); ADR-0037; `docs/specification/02_Architecture/05_PIPELINE_ENGINE.md#qa-checks`, `#qa-thresholds`, `#confidence` (the margins, which task 0.3 records there); `modules/pipeline/src/main/java/ua/bookloom/pipeline/qa/LengthBand.java` and `modules/pipeline/src/main/java/ua/bookloom/pipeline/DisplayText.java` (task 7.3).
  - **Change:** in package `ua.bookloom.pipeline.qa` (created by task 7.3): `CheckName` enum carrying each soft check's confidence weight as a constructor field (GLOSSARY 0.30, LENGTH 0.25, SCRIPT 0.20, ECHO 0.15, REPETITION 0.10; the hard gates REFUSAL, PLACEHOLDER, LOCKED_TERM and KEPT_RUN with none — the last two being the two outcomes of task 9.3's one protected-span gate) and the finding type it raises (SCRIPT and ECHO `language`, REPETITION `fluency`, LENGTH `omission`, GLOSSARY `glossary`, REFUSAL `meaning`, PLACEHOLDER `markup`, LOCKED_TERM `glossary`, KEPT_RUN `markup`); `CheckResult` record (`CheckName`, `margin`, `passed`, `skipped`, `blocking`, `@Nullable QaFinding`) — a failed soft check is `blocking` with a `medium` finding, except a failed echo whose source display text, after the name removal below, has fewer than 20 code points, which is not blocking and carries a `low` `language` finding; `TextSimilarity` (NFC via `java.text.Normalizer`, `toLowerCase(Locale.ROOT)`, `1 − Levenshtein / max length`); `ScriptCheck`, `EchoCheck` (owning the echo floor), `RepetitionCheck`, `LengthCheck` (reading `LengthBand`) and `GlossaryCheck`, run in that fixed order by `SoftChecks.run(SoftCheckInput)`; `SoftCheckInput` carrying the source and target display texts (`DisplayText.of` over the masked texts, so every document and protected token is already gone), the brief's source language (`@Nullable`) and the target language, the `ForeignPassagePolicy`, the `NamePolicy`, the segment's `@Nullable` declared block language, the glossary's terms (for the keep-original removal) and a `List<LockedRendering(term, rendering)>` of the locked terms present in the segment. Pass, fail and margins as design D8 fixes them (task 0.3 states the same in `05_PIPELINE_ENGINE.md#confidence`): a failed check 0.0, a skipped one 1.0; script — skipped when the source display text has fewer than 20 code points, the two languages share a script or the target has no letters, fails a share below 0.60 of the target's letters in the target language's script, margin `clamp((share − 0.60) / 0.20)`; echo — fails at a similarity of 0.90 or more, margin `clamp((0.90 − similarity) / 0.10)`; repetition — fails a sequence of 3 words repeated 3 or more times in a row, margin 0.5 for exactly two in a row, else 1.0; length — fails outside the pair's band (`LengthBand.forPair(…).widenedFor(sourceChars)`), margin `clamp(distance to the nearer bound / (0.10 × band width))`; glossary — skipped with no locked term present, fails when any present locked term's rendering is missing from the target, else 1.0. Under `NamePolicy.KEEP_ORIGINAL` every whole-word occurrence of a glossary term is removed from both display texts before the script share, the echo similarity and the echo floor are computed. A segment is marked foreign only under `ForeignPassagePolicy.KEEP`, when its declared block language, normalized, differs from the brief's normalized source language, or when the dominant script of its source display text differs from the source language's; a marked segment skips echo and script; under any other policy nothing is marked.
  - **Test first:** `SoftChecksTest`, one `@ParameterizedTest` per check with every spec scenario as a row and the margin written out: en→uk `He opened the old door.` → `Він відчинив старі двері.` script passes 1.0, → `Vin vidchynyv stari dveri.` fails 0.0 (blocking, medium `language`); en→pl `Otworzył stare drzwi.` and the Czech `Otevřel staré dveře.` skip 1.0; `Yes, sir.` (9 code points) skips 1.0; under `KEEP_ORIGINAL` with the glossary `Hale`, `Margaret`, `Milton`, `London`, the target `Hale і Margaret Hale виїхали з Milton до London рано того холодного ранку.` for `Hale and Margaret Hale left Milton for London early that cold morning.` has share 1.0 and passes, and under `TRANSLITERATE` the same target's share is 0.54 and fails; echo: the verbatim copy and `HE OPENED THE OLD DOOR.` fail 0.0, blocking with a medium `language` finding; `It was a dark night.` → `IT WAS A DARK NIGHT.` (exactly 20 code points) fails blocking; `Yes, sir.` → `YES, SIR.` fails 0.0, not blocking, with a low `language` finding; the Ukrainian target passes 1.0; under `KEEP_ORIGINAL` with the glossary `Margaret Hale`, `Milton Northern`, the unchanged `Margaret Hale, Milton Northern.` fails the echo without blocking; repetition: `двері відчинилися знову` three times in a row fails 0.0 (blocking, medium `fluency`), `двері відчинилися знову двері відчинилися знову і він увійшов` passes 0.5; length en→uk 100→50 characters fails (blocking, medium `omission`), 100→115 passes 1.0, 200→151 (ratio 0.755) passes 0.5, `Go.`→`Іди-но звідси.` fails in the widened band 0.35–3.6 and `Go.`→`Йди.` passes, uk→el ratio 2.3 passes in the default band 0.5–2.5; glossary: `Hale`→`Гейл` and `Milton`→`Мілтон` both rendered 1.0, only one of the two rendered fails 0.0 with a medium `glossary` finding, no locked term skipped 1.0, the unlocked `Baker Street` rendered `вулиця Бейкер` 1.0; foreign: KEEP with block language `fr` and `Je ne regrette rien.` returned unchanged skips echo and script, KEEP and `Γνῶθι σεαυτόν` unchanged in an English book skips both, KEEP and the unmarked `He opened the old door.` unchanged fails echo, KEEP with block language `en-GB` and source `en` is not marked, KEEP with source `en` and no block language is not marked even for a book whose package declares `uk` (the input carries only the brief's source), TRANSLATE with block `fr` unchanged fails echo. Unit tier.
  - **Log:** none inside the checks — they are pure and run per segment; task 8.6 logs one DEBUG line per evaluated segment with each check's margin.
  - **Done when:** `./gradlew :pipeline:test --tests 'ua.bookloom.pipeline.qa.*'` is green and `:pipeline`'s coverage check passes.

The reference numbers keep their values and now mean: en→uk `He opened the old door.` → `HE OPENED THE OLD DOOR.` has confidence 0.65 and fails echo and script outright, so it is never accepted in any mode and ends FLAGGED after its repairs (task 8.6); `Yes, sir.` → `YES, SIR.` has confidence 0.85 with an echo failure below the floor, so it is accepted in every mode.

One judge call per chunk keeps Balanced affordable and lets the judge see neighbouring sentences, and short local labels are easier for a small model to keep straight than segment ids. → `:pipeline`

  - **Change:** `memory.TranslationMemory` over `TmRepository`: `contextKey(Segment, List<Segment> unitSegments)` = the `HashUtil` SHA-256 of the previous neighbour's `sourceHash` (or `⟦BOS⟧` for a unit's first segment), `|` and the next neighbour's `sourceHash` (or `⟦EOS⟧` for its last), the neighbours taken from the unit's segments in document order — never `prevKey`/`nextKey`; `entryFor(Segment, List<Segment> unitSegments, String maskedTarget)` → the `TmEntry` the job commits for an ACCEPTED segment (id `<sourceHash>:<contextKey>`, `sourceInner` the source's display text, `targetInner` the masked target, so a reuse restores through the same gate); `lookup(Segment, List<Segment> unitSegments)` → `TmLookup(@Nullable TmEntry reuse, List<TmEntry> hints, List<TmEntry> suggestions)` — reuse from `TmRepository.context`, hints the other `exact` entries of the same source hash, suggestions the `TmRepository.candidates` in the length band `[ceil(0.85 × len), floor(len / 0.85)]` of the source display text whose similarity (`TextSimilarity` over the NFC case-folded display texts) is at least 0.85, never duplicating a hint, highest first. Nothing here writes to the repository: entries reach it only through a commit, so a review edit never changes the memory.
  - **Test first:** `TranslationMemoryTest` over the in-memory repository: `ch03.xhtml:12` `Yes.` between the same two sentences that surrounded `ch01.xhtml:4`, whose entry for `Так.` was put in the repository → reuse `Так.` although the two segments' positional ids differ; `ch05.xhtml:2` `Yes.` between different neighbours → no reuse, hint `Так.`; `ch04.xhtml:0`, the first segment of its unit, reading `Yes.` and followed by the sentence that followed `ch01.xhtml:4` (not a first segment) → no reuse, hint `Так.`; a unit's first segment matches a stored unit-first segment with the same next neighbour (`⟦BOS⟧`); stored `He opened the old doors.` and segment `He opened the old door.` → suggestion; a stored source of similarity 0.70 → nothing; a stored source outside the length band is never scored; `entryFor` gives the same key for the same text between the same neighbours in two different units. Unit tier.
  - **Log:** DEBUG each lookup: segment id, reuse found, hint count, suggestion count; DEBUG each entry built: segment id; no book text above TRACE.
  - **Done when:** `./gradlew :pipeline:test --tests 'ua.bookloom.pipeline.memory.TranslationMemoryTest'` is green.

The model forgets earlier chapters, so a short record of who is who travels with every prompt; the deterministic one costs nothing, the model-written one a call per chapter. → `:pipeline`

## 10. Job, review, export and the command line — after groups 8 and 9 (and 6, whose auxiliary kinds the run translates or keeps as source by switch)

- [ ] 10.1 Migrate the job's contract to a run over a stored project, changing no translation decision: `RunRequest` in place of `TranslationRequest`, `JobStage` `PREP`/`TRANSLATE`/`REVISE` with no export stage, `JobReport` without `written`, each decision stored through `CheckpointPort` as it is made, a start at the project's first pending segment, the run's state written to `RunRepository` through `run.RunRecorder`, the auxiliary unit still left out of the work and the counts, the book writer moved unchanged behind a minimal `ExportService`, and every caller in `:ui` and `:app` migrated minimally — with an interim bridge that still exports after a window run. Review, export, retry and resume all need the run's decisions stored in one place instead of inside the job, a stopped run can only be resumed if it starts where the stored decisions end, and doing the migration with today's acceptance keeps every existing count as it is, so the tasks that change what a run decides (10.2, 10.3) are measured against a green, unchanged baseline (ADR-0034, ADR-0035). → `:api`, `:pipeline`, `:ui`, `:app`
  - **Depends on:** tasks 1.2–1.6, 2.1–2.4, 6.2 (the auxiliary unit and the interim skip this task carries into the repository reads), 7.6 (`ProjectServiceImpl`, `OpenProjects`).
  - **Excludes:** the `PREP` stage, chunk packing, the judge, the quality loop and the "Also translate" switches — task 10.2 (here `SegmentTranslator` drafts one segment at a time and accepts a reply whose markup restores, exactly as today, so every count holds); memory reuse, the context package, protected spans, the summary, names grown at a unit's end, deferrals and `MemoryUpdated` — task 10.3; review pause points, the provider-error routing and the interrupt fix — task 10.4 (this task keeps today's model-error handling); the new live events and throughput fields — task 10.5; the `REVISE` stage — task 10.7; side files, the consistency pass, per-segment verification and the source-changed check's new wording — task 10.8 (here the moved writer keeps exactly today's temporary-file → re-open → count → atomic-move chain); the command line's destination preflight and Ctrl+C — task 10.10; the review-mode flag and the remaining bindings — task 10.11; the `:ui` runner's real rework on the new API — tasks 11.3–11.5. In `:ui` and `:app` this task makes only the smallest change that keeps both compiling and their existing tests green. The automatic export after a completed window run that this task adds to `TranslationRunner` is an interim bridge that keeps today's window tests meaningful; task 11.3 deletes it, and from then on only the Export screen writes the book.
  - **Read:** `specs/translation-pipeline/spec.md` "Send each pending segment to the model in document order" ("A second run continues where the first stopped"), "Refuse a run that cannot start" (all three scenarios), "Report progress and the outcome" (no book written, no export stage, "A failed job still returns its report"), "Stop the job on any other failure" (today's behaviour, kept until task 10.4); `specs/resume/spec.md` "Cancel a job" ("Decisions survive a cancel"), "Pause at enabled pause points" ("Pause between stages": no file written), "Pause on request at the next boundary" ("A pause after the last decision"), "Record whether a run is running, paused or ended" (all three scenarios); `specs/review-queue/spec.md` "Move segments only through the segment status machine" ("A flagged segment is not picked up again by the run"); `specs/book-brief/spec.md` "Carry every brief choice into the run"; design D2 (commits, the run record, kept as source by choice), D3, D12 (the interim rule for the auxiliary unit), "Migration Plan"; ADR-0034, ADR-0035; every file naming `TranslationRequest`: `modules/api/src/main/java/ua/bookloom/api/pipeline/{TranslationRequest,TranslationEngine,JobStage,JobReport,PausePoint}.java`; `modules/pipeline/src/main/java/ua/bookloom/pipeline/{TranslationJobImpl,TranslationEngineImpl,JobLifecycleLogger,TranslationJobRequestContext,TranslationJobErrors,JobProgressTracker,SegmentTranslator,BookExporter,ExportMoveOperation,ExportPathAliases}.java`; the tests `modules/pipeline/src/test/java/ua/bookloom/pipeline/{TranslationJobTestSupport,TranslationJob*Test,TranslationEngine*Test,BookExporter*Test,DiagnosticsTranslationJobTest}.java`; `modules/ui/src/main/java/ua/bookloom/ui/state/{BookBriefViewModel,TranslatingViewModel,TranslationRunner,RunSession}.java` and `modules/ui/src/test/java/ua/bookloom/ui/{ScriptedTranslationEngine.java,state/TranslatingViewModelStartTest.java,state/BookBriefViewModelTestBase.java,state/BookBriefViewModelRequestTest.java,state/RunnerTestBase.java,screen/BookBriefScreenDisabledCardsTest.java}`, `modules/ui/src/test/java/ua/bookloom/ui/UiTestInjector.java` (it binds every port explicitly, `:197–209`); `modules/app/src/main/java/ua/bookloom/app/cli/TranslateCommand.java`, `modules/app/src/test/java/ua/bookloom/app/TranslationWorkspaceEndToEndTest.java`.
  - **Change:** `:api` — delete `TranslationRequest`; `TranslationEngine.newJob(RunRequest, ChatModel)`; `JobStage` becomes `PREP`, `TRANSLATE`, `REVISE` (`EXPORT` removed); `JobReport` loses `written` and its "completed implies written" invariant; `PausePoint.BETWEEN_STAGES` documented as "after translation, before revision or the end". `:pipeline` — `TranslationEngineImpl.newJob` only null-checks and builds the job; the job's `run()` refuses without a model call: already run, unknown project (`ErrorCode.validation`), a brief target or source language failing today's language-code pattern (`ErrorCode.validation`), no opened document for the project — its book is no longer open (`ErrorCode.validation`). The work list is the project's PENDING records in document order, starting at `firstPending(projectId, kept)`, where `kept` is, until task 10.2 replaces it with the brief's switches, every auxiliary kind (`new AlsoTranslate(false, false, false, false).keptKinds()`) — so the auxiliary unit stays out of the work, the pending count and the section count exactly as task 6.2's tracker left it; `JobProgressTracker` counts from `countsByStatus(projectId, kept)`. Each segment is drafted and decided by today's `SegmentTranslator` and its decision stored at once as a one-segment `CheckpointPort.commit(ChunkCommit)` (status, machine target in plain and masked form, confidence, findings, path `DRAFT`), before the boundary that follows it — so what is decided is always what is stored and a cancel keeps every decision. New package `ua.bookloom.pipeline.run` with `package-info.java` and `run.RunRecorder`, which writes the run's `RunRecord` through `RunRepository.save` (an upsert by the job's id): `RUNNING` with `startedAt` when the run starts, `PAUSED` at every pause and `RUNNING` again at every resume, then `COMPLETED`, `CANCELLED` or `FAILED` with `endedAt` and the counts; its times come from a `java.time.Clock` that `PipelineModule` provides (`Clock.systemUTC()`) and the engine hands to each job. Move `BookExporter`, `ExportMoveOperation`, `ExportPathAliases` and the engine's file-type and path-identity checks into package `ua.bookloom.pipeline.export` behind a minimal `ExportServiceImpl`/`ExportJobImpl` that writes, from a fresh open, each record's effective target (ACCEPTED and REVISED their target, FLAGGED its machine target when it has one, everything else its source) — the chain itself unchanged; `module-info` gains `opens ua.bookloom.pipeline.export to com.google.guice;` and `opens ua.bookloom.pipeline.run to com.google.guice;`; `PipelineModule` binds `ProjectService` and `ExportService`, which the migrated callers now need. `:ui` — a `ui.state` record carrying the five values `TranslationRequest` held replaces it in `BookBriefViewModel.request()`; `TranslatingViewModel.prepare` imports the source through `ProjectService` (interim — task 11.3 removes this call so start and resume reuse the project id the Import screen produced), sets the brief's languages, and creates the job with `RunRequest(projectId, ReviewMode.UNATTENDED)`; A run without these forgets who is who from one chapter to the next, misspells a locked name, translates a repeated line differently each time and leaves backward revision nothing to fix; each part was built and proven alone in group 9, and this task is where they meet the chunk lifecycle — the glossary read once per chunk, the reuse decided in its turn, the new names committed with the unit's last decisions (owner decision D-5). → `:pipeline`

`run_lockedTerm_maskedAndRestored` — `Hale` locked as `Гейл` and Markdown `Hale opened the *old* door.` → the draft request holds `⟦g2⟧ opened the ⟦g0⟧old⟦g1⟧ door.` and lists `⟦g2⟧ → Гейл, character, male`; the reply `⟦g2⟧ відчинив ⟦g0⟧старі⟦g1⟧ двері.` is stored with machine target `Гейл відчинив *старі* двері.` and masked machine target `Гейл відчинив ⟦g0⟧старі⟦g1⟧ двері.`; `run_keptForeignRun_sentAsOnePlaceholder` — Keep as-is, source `en`, EPUB `She whispered <i xml:lang="fr">au revoir</i> and left.` → the draft request holds `She whispered ⟦g2⟧ and left.`, and the reply `Вона прошепотіла ⟦g2⟧ і пішла.` is stored as `Вона прошепотіла <i xml:lang="fr">au revoir</i> і пішла.`; `run_summary_refreshedAfterTwentyAndAtUnitEnd` — a Fast Markdown chapter of 21 short segments, the first the heading `# The Lighthouse` → `MemoryUpdated(SUMMARY, …)` after the 20th accepted decision and at the chapter's end, no `SUMMARY` request, and the first draft carries no summary block while the 21st carries one; `run_maxUnitEnd_makesOneSummaryCall` — a Max run over a two-segment chapter → exactly one `SUMMARY` request, after the chapter's last decision; `run_unitEndScan_addsRecurringName` — the glossary holds only the person's locked `Hale` → `Гейл`, `Moreau` occurs twice mid-sentence in each of two chapters → nothing added after the first chapter, `Moreau` (unlocked, no target, other, unknown) committed with the second chapter's last decision and one `MemoryUpdated(GLOSSARY, "+1")`, `Hale` → `Гейл` unchanged; `run_unitEndScan_skipsRemovedTerm` — `Chapter` removed by the person during a pause → no later scan adds it; `run_prepScanAddingNames_announcesThem` — an empty glossary and `Hale` three times mid-sentence → one `MemoryUpdated(GLOSSARY, "+1")` from `PREP`, and a book naming nobody three times → none; `run_judgeDeferral_storedWithCommit` — a Balanced chunk judged with a deferral on `s2` → an open JUDGE deferral on the chunk's second segment after its commit; `run_unknownGender_recordsDeferral` — the glossary holds `Sam` (character, unknown) and a decided segment mentions `Sam` → an open GENDER_UNKNOWN deferral on it; `run_flaggedSegment_writesNoMemoryEntry` — a FLAGGED segment's commit carries no TM entry; `run_threeSegments_sendsOnlyTheSummaryUpdate` — TXT `One.`, `Two.`, `Three.` on Fast → exactly one `MemoryUpdated`, `SUMMARY`, at the end of the file. Pipeline e2e tier.

The book `Book.md`, five paragraphs: `The door was opened by Hale at night.`, `She walked with Hale into the dark hall.`, `They waited for *Hale* until the morning.`, `He left the house at dawn.`, `The rain did not stop that evening.`; brief en→uk on Balanced with an empty glossary; run Assisted with pause points on-flagged and on-error. `modules/pipeline/src/test/java/ua/bookloom/pipeline/WholeBookPipelineLiveTest.java` (created by this task), `@Tag("liveLocal")`, one case per dialect gated by `@EnabledIfEnvironmentVariable` on `BOOKLOOM_LIVE_OLLAMA_URL` and on `BOOKLOOM_LIVE_LMSTUDIO_URL`, the model from `BOOKLOOM_LIVE_OLLAMA_MODEL` / `BOOKLOOM_LIVE_LMSTUDIO_MODEL`, the same book on Balanced, Unattended.

Assertions: `PREP` proposes `Hale` (three mid-sentence occurrences), unlocked with no target, and sends one `MemoryUpdated(GLOSSARY, "+1")`; after exactly 9 requests the run pauses on-flagged naming `Book.md:3`, with `Book.md:0` … `Book.md:3` committed; `Book.md:1` is ACCEPTED with path REPAIRED; `ReviewDesk.saveEdit` of `Він вийшов з дому на світанку.` makes `Book.md:3` REVISED; after resume request 10 (draft `Того вечора дощ так і не вщух.` for `Book.md:4`, the first segment of the next chunk) carries `Він вийшов з дому на світанку.` as a preceding target and request 11 (judge `{"score":0.92,"verdict":"accept","findings":[],"deferrals":[]}`) decides it; the unit's end adds no glossary entry (`Hale` is already held) and sends one `MemoryUpdated(SUMMARY, …)` with no summary request; the run ends Completed with 11 requests in all and the run record reading `COMPLETED`; one `ModelCallFinished` carries the stubbed usage, not estimated; exporting with all three side files writes `Book.uk.md` holding `Двері відчинив Гейл пізно вночі.`, `Вона пройшла з Гейлом до темної зали.`, `Вони чекали на *Гейла* до самого ранку.`, `Він вийшов з дому на світанку.` and `Того вечора дощ так і не вщух.`, `Book.uk.glossary.csv` starting `term,target,type,gender,locked` with a row starting `Hale,`, `Book.uk.bilingual.html` with 5 segment rows and no `http`, and `Book.uk.report.md` naming the counts. The 503 case: Fast, Unattended with on-error, `He opened the old door.` and `She left the house early.`; stubs `{"target":"Він відчинив старі двері."}`, `503`, `503`, `503`, `{"target":"Вона рано вийшла з дому."}` → pause with `ErrorCode.upstream`, 1 accepted and 1 pending after 4 requests; resume → Completed, 2 accepted, 5 requests. The `liveLocal` twin: run once with the variables unset and see both cases skipped; against real servers each ends Completed with no PENDING segment, every draft reply parsed (a structural repair allowed) and at least one judge reply readable.
  - **Log:** the run's own lines; read the test log once at TRACE and confirm no book text above TRACE.

## 11. UI plumbing — after group 10 (task 11.1 needs nothing from it)

- [ ] 11.1 Add a searchable choice box, `ui.control.SearchableCombo<T>`, that narrows a fixed list to the entries whose displayed name contains the typed text anywhere, ignoring case and accents, in a strict mode that keeps the previous value when the typed text matches nothing and a free-text mode that keeps what was typed. The language lists grow to 34 entries and the genre list to 40; without this control a person scrolls through them, and without the strict mode a half-typed `Klingon` would become a target language. → `:ui`
  - **Depends on:** nothing beyond the existing `:ui` module; it can be built at any time after group 1 and keeps its place here with the rest of the UI plumbing.
  - **Excludes:** the lists themselves and their display names (task 11.8), and wiring the control into the Book Brief (task 12.2). ControlsFX `SearchableComboBox` is not used: it cannot take free text and is unproven in the packaged image (`design.md` D15).
  - **Read:** `specs/app-shell/spec.md` "Search any fixed list by typing"; `specs/book-brief/spec.md` "Offer one searchable list of languages for source and target" (the unmatched-text scenario); `design.md` D1 (the `ua.bookloom.ui.control` package), D15; `docs/specification/01_Product/08_UI_SCREENS_AND_STATES.md#control-mapping-summary`; `modules/ui/src/main/java/module-info.java` (the existing `opens` lines to copy); `modules/ui/src/main/resources/ua/bookloom/ui/theme.css` (`.combo-box`, `.combo-box-popup`).
  - **Change:** create the package `ua.bookloom.ui.control` with a `package-info.java` (`@NullMarked`, a purpose Javadoc) and the line `opens ua.bookloom.ui.control to com.google.guice, javafx.fxml;` in `module-info.java`. `SearchableCombo<T>` extends an editable `ComboBox<T>` over a `FilteredList<T>` whose predicate compares the editor text with each entry's displayed name after `java.text.Normalizer` NFD decomposition, removal of combining marks and `Locale.ROOT` lower-casing; it takes a `Function<T, String>` for the displayed name and a `boolean allowFreeText`, and exposes the committed value as a read-only property. On focus loss: strict mode restores the value held before typing began when the text names no entry; free-text mode commits the typed text through a `Function<String, T>` supplied by the caller. Styling reuses `.combo-box`; no new colour.
  - **Test first:** `SearchableComboTest` (TestFX, headless) over the 34 English language names written out in the test: typing `ukr` leaves exactly `Ukrainian`; `fran` includes `French`; `BOKMAL` includes `Norwegian Bokmål`; `chin` leaves `Chinese (Simplified)` and `Chinese (Traditional)`; in strict mode with `Ukrainian` chosen, typing `xyz` and moving focus away leaves `Ukrainian`; in free-text mode typing `solarpunk novella` and moving focus away commits `solarpunk novella`; choosing an entry from the narrowed list commits that entry and clears the filter. A `@ParameterizedTest` over (`typed`, `expectedFirst`) pairs, no loops in the body.

- [ ] 11.5 Route each pause and failure of a run to its surface: a pause on error becomes the provider-error state whatever its code, a review pause names its segment, `FailureSurface` places each of the fifteen codes met outside a run's own outcome, and `Controls.of` offers in each state exactly the controls that starting and resuming allow. Without this a provider hiccup reads as a crash instead of a pause the person can resume, a `busy` refusal of a review retry opens a blocking dialog, and a paused or stopped run offers a Start that would begin a second run beside the one that can be resumed. → `:ui`
  - **Depends on:** task 11.4 (the `RunSession` dispatch this task adds its pause cases to, and the `info` and `retry` entries a pause and its resume already log); task 11.3 (the resume path — the same job after a pause, a new job after a stop — and `Controls.of`'s `STOPPED` row); task 11.2 (the mirror's `providerError` and `reviewPauseSegment`); task 10.4 (`Paused` naming the segment and carrying the error, and the pipeline's routing of each model-call code, design D3).
  - **Excludes:** the provider-error banner and its Retry now, Open provider settings and Stay paused actions, and reading the pending count that tells a COMPLETED run's screen to offer Start — task 13.2; opening the review panel on a review pause — task 13.4; the title bar's state text — task 11.6; the review retry that meets `busy` — task 13.3 (here only the surface `busy` gets).
  - **Read:** `specs/resume/spec.md` "Pause instead of failing when an error stops the job", "Pause for review as the review mode says" (the pause names its segment); `specs/notifications/spec.md` "Report a provider failure as the run's own state", "Decide the surface from the failure's own code"; `specs/app-shell/spec.md` "Move through the workflow by its available steps" (when a start is offered); `specs/translation-pipeline/spec.md` "Show the Translating screen's idle, completed and failed states", "Learn the run's outcome even when no event is sent"; `specs/review-queue/spec.md` "Allow a retry whenever no run of the project is running" (the `busy` refusal); `design.md` D3 (failure routing), D15 (start and resume, the Translating states); `modules/ui/src/main/java/ua/bookloom/ui/state/{FailureSurface,Controls,ControlState,RunNotice,RunSession,TranslatingViewModel}.java`; `modules/ui/src/test/java/ua/bookloom/ui/state/{FailureSurfaceTest,ControlsTest,RunSessionTest}.java`, `modules/ui/src/test/java/ua/bookloom/ui/{RecordingErrorPresenter,RecordingToasts}.java`. Also `modules/ui/src/main/java/ua/bookloom/ui/screen/TranslatingView.java` (`Controls::newRun` at `:70`), `modules/ui/src/main/java/ua/bookloom/ui/i18n/MessageKey.java` (`TRANSLATING_NEW_RUN`) with its two catalogue entries, `modules/ui/src/test/java/ua/bookloom/ui/screen/TranslatingScreenTest.java` (the New run rows for COMPLETED and FAILED) and `modules/ui/src/test/java/ua/bookloom/ui/screen/TranslatingScreenErrorTest.java` (runs failed with a provider code, asserting a banner and no dialog).
  - **Change:** `RunSession`: `Paused` with reason `ON_ERROR` publishes its error as the mirror's provider error — every pause on error is the provider-error state whatever its code, so LM Studio's `Model unloaded` `validation` reaches it through the pause — and the next `Resumed` clears it; `Paused` with `ON_FLAGGED` or `AFTER_SEGMENT` publishes its segment id as the review-pause segment, cleared by the next `Resumed`. `FailureSurface.of(code)` follows the notifications table for a failure met outside a run's own outcome: `unreachable`, `timeout`, `auth`, `rateLimited`, `upstream`, `modelNotFound`, `modelUnavailable` and `missingCredential` → `PROVIDER_ERROR`; `contextWindow` and `emptyCompletion` → a new `FLAGGED_SEGMENT` (no surface of their own; the run goes on); `busy` → a new `WARNING_TOAST` (only a review retry refused while the project's run is running, task 13.3); `cancelled` → `STOPPED`; `validation` → `IN_PLACE`; `internal` → `DIALOG`; `discoveryFailed` → `SETTINGS_ONLY` (only a model listing). A run's own outcome is routed by its state, not by `FailureSurface.of` (design D3): a CANCELLED run is the stopped state; a FAILED run is the blocking dialog whatever code it carries — the pipeline reports a `busy` or `discoveryFailed` reaching a run as `internal`, and the window never shows either as a warning toast or a settings note. A `validation` refusing a start stays `IN_PLACE`, and the `RunNotice.ProviderError` path of a failed preparation stays. `Controls.of(state, preparing, pendingRemain)` follows design D15 and app-shell's "Move through the workflow by its available steps": `IDLE` — the project has no run — offers the start; `RUNNING` pause and stop; `PAUSING` stop; `PAUSED`, a review pause and a pause on error alike, resume and stop; `STOPPING` nothing; `STOPPED` resume only (task 11.3's row); `COMPLETED` the start when `pendingRemain`, and nothing otherwise; `FAILED` the start. A start after a run is the same Start as before one, so the `newRun` control is folded into `start`, and no state offers both a start and Resume. `TranslatingViewModel` passes `pendingRemain` from a property that stays false until task 13.2 reads the project's pending count; its `start()` already starts nothing the controls do not offer. A FAILED outcome is told apart by its code: FAILED with `ErrorCode.validation` is a refused start and stays `IN_PLACE`; FAILED with any other code opens the blocking dialog. Folding `newRun` into `start` removes `TranslatingView`'s new-run control, `MessageKey.TRANSLATING_NEW_RUN` and its two catalogue entries; `TranslatingScreenTest`'s COMPLETED and FAILED rows expect the start control instead, and `TranslatingScreenErrorTest`'s runs failed with a provider code are rewritten to assert the dialog — the banner for a pause on error is owned by task 13.2's tests.
  - **Test first:** `RunSessionTest`: `Paused(ON_ERROR, unreachable)` publishes the provider error and the state PAUSED, and the next `Resumed` clears it; `Paused(ON_ERROR, validation)` from `Model unloaded` publishes the provider error just the same; `Paused(ON_FLAGGED)` naming `ch05.xhtml:11` publishes that id as the review-pause segment, and so does `Paused(AFTER_SEGMENT)` naming `ch01.xhtml:2`, each cleared by the next `Resumed`; `validation` both ways — from an `ON_ERROR` pause → the provider-error state, from a refused start → in place with no provider error; a FAILED run carrying `busy` or `discoveryFailed` shows the blocking dialog through `RecordingErrorPresenter` and raises no warning toast through `RecordingToasts`. `FailureSurfaceTest` over all fifteen codes at the surfaces listed above (a `@CsvSource`). `ControlsTest` over every state (a `@ParameterizedTest` with the offered controls written out): `IDLE` offers Start; `PAUSED` offers Resume and Stop and no Start; `STOPPED` offers Resume and no Start; `COMPLETED` offers nothing with `pendingRemain` false and Start with it true; `FAILED` offers Start; `preparing` disables Start. `TranslatingViewModelTest`: while the run is paused on a provider error, `start()` asks the recording engine for no job. `FailureSurfaceTest`/`RunSessionTest`: a FAILED run carrying `ErrorCode.validation` from a refused start shows the in-place message and no dialog; a FAILED run carrying `ErrorCode.unreachable` or `ErrorCode.internal` opens one dialog.
  - **Log:** DEBUG each pause with its reason and segment id, each surface chosen with its code, and each change of the offered controls with the state; no WARN or ERROR here — the failure was logged once where its `AppError` was built.
  - **Done when:** `./gradlew :ui:test --tests 'ua.bookloom.ui.state.*'` and `./gradlew :ui:check :app:test :app:archTest` are green, and in `./gradlew :app:run` a real run with Ollama stopped mid-run logs an `info` pause entry and stays paused instead of failing, and once Ollama is back Resume logs a `retry` entry and the run continues.

Group headings render `messages.get(heading).toUpperCase(messages.locale())`, because JavaFX CSS has no text transform and no letter spacing. `NavColumn` ends with a footer line (id `nav-footer`, existing muted navigation tokens) bound to `SettingsViewModel`'s selection: the selected provider's display name and the chosen model id joined by ` · `, or `No model chosen` when no model is chosen; `ProviderNames` becomes public so the column can name the provider, and no readiness dot is drawn (a stated deviation). New keys in both catalogues for the footer labels, the navigation footer's empty state and the Names & style heading, subtitle and banner.

  - **Change:** `docs/implementation_plan/01_MODULE_INVENTORY.md` — the new packages in `:api`, `:util`, `:persistence`, `:document`, `:pipeline` and `:ui`, with rows for `pipeline.run`, `document.inspect`, `document.split` and `persistence.memory` among them; the `pipeline.chunk` row corrected, because sentence splitting lives in `document.split` (`IcuSentenceSplitter` behind the `:api` `SentenceSplitter` port) and `:pipeline` holds no ICU; and `:persistence` holding in-memory adapters, no database. `AGENTS.md` "Where it stands" — the full workflow in the window, review inside Translating, export as its own action, nothing persisted across a restart; next comes SQLite persistence. `CHANGE_BACKLOG.md` — the consumed rows; D5, D7 (the pair rules adopted, ADR-0040 amended), D8, D17 and D19 closed, D18 partly (which reader/writer methods still lack DEBUG lines). `docs/next_features.md` — each fixed item marked with this change's name, §9 closed because the pair rules were adopted, the rest left open. `docs/specification/IMPLEMENTED_REQUIREMENTS.md` — a section for `complete-translation-workflow` naming each FR id it implements and the capability that owns it, and recording in plain words the owner decisions it built: D-2 (only the text values of Markdown frontmatter are segments, and an existing `lang` value is replaced on export), D-4 (a TXT or Markdown export the source encoding cannot hold is written as UTF-8), D-5 (names grow at each chapter's end) and D-6 (an inline foreign passage is kept verbatim under the Keep policy) — prose only, no coverage script. `docs/DEVELOPMENT.md#running` — the `BOOKLOOM_REVIEW_MODE` / `bookloom.review.mode` flag and its values, and what the app can now do. Run `OPENSPEC_TELEMETRY=0 openspec validate complete-translation-workflow --strict`.
  - **Log:** none.
  - **Done when:** no document contradicts the shipped behaviour, `OPENSPEC_TELEMETRY=0 openspec validate complete-translation-workflow --strict` is clean and, closing the group, `./gradlew build :app:archTest` is still green.
